 10: 패턴 스캔 이전에 단락 단위 burstiness와 어휘 다양성(MATTR)을 계산하고, 의심 단락 내부에서
 11: 문장 단위로 zoom-in 한다. 결과는 LLM에게 내부 작업 메모리로 전달되며 사용자 출력에는 노출하지 않는다.

 14: 
 15: ## 1. Overview
 16: 
 17: `core/scoring.md`가 패턴 감지 결과를 0-100 점수로 환산하는 채점 알고리즘이라면,
 18: 이 문서는 패턴 스캔 *이전에* 동작하는 통계 전처리 알고리즘을 정의한다.
 19: 
 20: 목적:
 21: - 28개 패턴 카탈로그가 직접 명명하지 못하는 분포적 단서(균질한 문장 길이, 빈약한
 22:   어휘 다양성)를 결정론적 수치로 잡아낸다
 23: - 4.5단계 의미 앵커(Semantic Anchor)와 같은 "내부 작업 메모리" 패턴을 따라,
 24:   hot 단락/문장 정보를 5a·5b 입력에 주입한다
 25: - 외부 의존성(형태소 분석기, 외부 detector API) 없이 ko/en은 whitespace 토큰화,
 26:   zh/ja는 deterministic character-token fallback으로 동작한다
 27: - ko는 dependency-free 보조 지표(띄어쓰기 규칙성, 쉼표 밀도, 조사/어미 suffix diversity
 28:   proxy)를 함께 기록하고, 세 지표가 모두 보수적 임계값을 넘을 때만 ko 전용 hot 신호로 사용한다
 29: 
 30: 이 알고리즘은 5a 구조 분석 단계와 5b 문장/어휘 단계에 자연스럽게 결합되도록 설계되었다.
 31: - 5a는 단락 수준 hot 표시를 우선 검토 대상으로 사용한다
 32: - 5b는 문장 수준 hot 그룹을 우선 재작성 대상으로 사용한다
 33: 
 34: ### 언어 범위
 35: 
 36: | 언어 | 지원 | 비고 |
 37: |------|---------|------|
 38: | ko | yes | 어절 단위 토큰화 + dependency-free diagnostic signals |
 39: | en | yes | 단어 단위 토큰화 |
 40: | zh | yes | Han character-token fallback |
 41: | ja | yes | Kana/Han character-token fallback; 형태소 분석 없음 |
 42: 
 43: 기본 `stylometry.languages`는 `[ko, en, zh, ja]`이다. 사용자가 설정에서 언어를 제거하면
 44: 해당 언어는 4.6단계를 skip 한다.

 48: ## 2. Tokenization Policy
 49: 
 50: 외부 의존성을 배제하기 위해 형태소 분석기 없이 토큰화한다. ko/en은 whitespace +
 51: edge-punctuation strip, zh/ja는 Han/Kana 문자와 ASCII run을 token으로 삼는 fallback을
 52: 사용한다. raw token만으로도 burstiness와 MATTR 신호는 충분히 안정적이다.
 53: 
 54: ### 절차
 55: 
 56: ```
 57: tokens = []
 58: IF language is zh or ja:
 59:     FOR each Han/Kana character or ASCII alnum run IN sentence:
 60:         tokens.append(match)
 61: ELSE:
 62:     FOR each whitespace-separated chunk IN sentence:
 63:         stripped = chunk with leading/trailing `\W` characters removed
 64:         IF stripped is non-empty:
 65:             tokens.append(stripped)
 66: ```
 67: 
 68: - `\W` 는 정규식의 비단어 문자 클래스 (Unicode aware: 한글·영문 단어 문자는 보존)
 69: - 내부 punctuation(예: `don't`, `좋은-도구`)은 token 일부로 유지한다
 70: - 빈 문자열은 token 으로 인정하지 않는다
 71: 
 72: ### 단위
 73: 
 74: | 언어 | 토큰 단위 | 예시 입력 | 토큰 |
 75: |------|-----------|-----------|------|
 76: | ko | 어절 (whitespace 기준) | `이 도구는 자동완성처럼 동작한다` | `[이, 도구는, 자동완성처럼, 동작한다]` |
 77: | en | 단어 (whitespace 기준) | `The tool acts like autocomplete.` | `[The, tool, acts, like, autocomplete]` |
 78: | zh | 한자 문자 | `工具提升写作` | `[工, 具, 提, 升, 写, 作]` |
 79: | ja | 가나/한자 문자 | `道具が書く` | `[道, 具, が, 書, く]` |
 80: 
 81: > **주의:** 한국어는 어절 기준이므로 morpheme-level 분석과 다르다. `도구는`과 `도구가`는
 82: > 서로 다른 토큰으로 취급된다. zh/ja 역시 형태소 단위가 아니라 문자 fallback이다. jieba,
 83: > sudachi, mecab 같은 형태소 분석기는 배포 의존성과 설치 실패면을 늘리므로 기본 경로에서
 84: > 제외한다.
 85: 
 86: ko diagnostic POS diversity는 형태소 분석기가 아니라 suffix proxy다. 조사/어미 후보 목록으로
 87: 어절 끝을 보수적으로 분류하고, class diversity와 coverage를 기록한다. 이 proxy는 진짜 POS
 88: tagger가 아니므로 단독 hot 신호가 아니라 spacing/comma와 결합된 ko composite에서만 사용한다.
 89: 
 90: ---
 91: 
 92: ## 3. Sentence Splitting
 93: 
 94: 문장 분할은 단순 정규식만 사용한다. 약어·소수점 등 false split 가능성은 v1에서 감수한다.
 95: 
 96: ### 규칙
 97: 
 98: ```
 99: split text on regex: [.!?。…]+\s+ OR \n+
100: trim whitespace
101: drop empty fragments
102: ```
103: 
104: 종결부호 후보:
105: - `.` (마침표)
106: - `!` (느낌표)
107: - `?` (물음표)
108: - `。` (전각 마침표 — 향후 ja/zh 확장 대비)
109: - `…` (말줄임표)
110: - 줄바꿈 `\n`
111: 
112: ### 단락 분할
113: 
114: - 빈 줄(`\n\s*\n`) 기준으로 단락 분리
115: - 단락 내부 줄바꿈은 같은 단락의 문장 경계로 취급
116: 
117: ---
118: 
119: ## 4. Burstiness Metric
120: 
121: 문장 길이의 변동성을 측정한다. AI 텍스트는 문장 길이가 균질해지는 경향이 있다.
122: 
123: ### 공식
124: 
125: ```
126: sentence_token_counts = [len(tokens) for sentence in paragraph.sentences]
127: mean = sum(sentence_token_counts) / len(sentence_token_counts)
128: stddev = sqrt(sum((x - mean)^2) / len(sentence_token_counts))   # population stddev
129: burstiness_CV = stddev / mean
130: ```
131: 
132: - `CV` = Coefficient of Variation (변동계수)
133: - mean 이 0 이면 정의되지 않음 → skip 처리
134: - population stddev 사용 (표본 보정 없음, 단순화)
135: - 단락 문장이 3개 미만이면 CV 값은 기록하되 burstiness band는 부여하지 않는다.
136:   2문장 이하 샘플은 길이 변동성이 너무 불안정해서 hot 판정에서 제외한다.
137: 
138: ### 밴드
139: 
140: | 밴드 | 임계값 | 해석 |
141: |------|--------|------|
142: | low | CV < 0.30 | 문장 길이가 균질함 — AI 의심 |
143: | mid | 0.30 ≤ CV ≤ 0.50 | 자연스러운 변동 |
144: | high | CV > 0.50 | 사람다운 큰 변동 |
145: 
146: 임계값은 `.patina.default.yaml`의 `stylometry.burstiness.bands`에서 조정 가능하다.
147: 
148: > **v3.5.1 calibration:** 초기 임계값은 `0.25`였다. 외부 300 단락
149: > (HC3 ChatGPT 100 + HC3 human 100 + Wikipedia 100) 측정 후 `0.30`으로 상향 조정.
150: > 0.25에서는 AI catch rate 가 57%에 그쳐 v1 목표(70%) 미달이었고,
151: > 0.30에서 66%까지 상승. 0.35 이상은 Wikipedia false positive 가 34%+ 로
152: > 급증해 채택하지 않음. 자세한 sweep 결과는 §13 참조.
153: 
154: ---
155: 
156: ## 5. TTR via MATTR
157: 
158: Type-Token Ratio (TTR)는 어휘 다양성 지표다. 텍스트 길이에 민감하므로 단순 TTR 대신
159: MATTR (Moving Average TTR)을 사용해 길이 의존성을 줄인다.
160: 
161: ### 공식
162: 
163: ```
164: window = 50  # tokens
165: lower_tokens = [token.lower() for token in paragraph_tokens]   # no stemming, no lemmatization
166: IF len(lower_tokens) < window:
167:     MATTR = len(set(lower_tokens)) / len(lower_tokens)         # fall back to simple TTR
168: ELSE:
169:     ratios = []
170:     FOR i in 0 .. len(lower_tokens) - window:
171:         slice = lower_tokens[i : i + window]
172:         ratios.append(len(set(slice)) / window)
173:     MATTR = sum(ratios) / len(ratios)
174: ```
175: 
176: - token normalization: lowercase 만 적용 (stemming/lemmatization 없음)
177: - 한국어 어절 token은 lowercase가 거의 영향 없음 (영문 외래어만 영향)
178: - window=50은 문헌 권장값 (Covington & McFall 2010)
179: 
180: ### 밴드
181: 
182: | 밴드 | 임계값 | 해석 |
183: |------|--------|------|
184: | low | MATTR < 0.55 | 어휘 반복 많음 — AI 의심 |
185: | mid | 0.55 ≤ MATTR ≤ 0.70 | 자연스러운 다양성 |
186: | high | MATTR > 0.70 | 풍부한 어휘 |
187: 
188: 임계값은 `.patina.default.yaml`의 `stylometry.ttr.bands`에서 조정 가능하다.
189: 
190: ---
191: 
192: ## 5.1 Korean Diagnostic Signals
193: 
194: 한국어는 어절 토큰화만으로는 조사/어미 변형과 띄어쓰기 습관을 충분히 보지 못한다. 그래서
195: `analyzeText(text, { lang: "ko" })`는 다음 보조 지표를 단락 결과에 추가한다.
196: 
197: | 필드 | 지표 | 목적 | 현재 판정 영향 |
198: |------|------|------|----------------|
199: | `spacing` | `eojeolLengthCV`, 평균 어절 길이, 1음절/긴 어절 비율 | LLM식으로 지나치게 균질한 띄어쓰기·어절 길이 후보 추적 | composite 조건 |
200: | `comma` | 쉼표 수, 문장당 쉼표, 100자당 쉼표 | 쉼표 리듬이 사라진 장문 후보 추적 | composite 조건 |
201: | `posDiversity` | 조사/어미 suffix class coverage/diversity proxy | 형태소 분석기 없이 기능어 다양성 후보 추적 | composite 조건 |
202: | `koDiagnostics` | 세 지표의 보수적 AND 판정 | burstiness/MATTR/lexicon이 놓치는 ko hotspot 보강 | hot OR-rule 참여 |
203: 
204: POS proxy 결정:
205: - 새 런타임 의존성은 추가하지 않는다.
206: - KoNLPy/mecab-ko/khaiii 같은 형태소 분석기는 설치 실패면과 배포 크기가 커서 기본 경로에서
207:   제외한다.
208: - suffix proxy는 `은/는`, `이/가`, `을/를`, `에게/에서`, `습니다/합니다` 같은 조사·어미
209:   class를 세고, `proxy: "suffix"`로 명시한다.
210: 
211: 운영 규칙:
212: - 세 조건을 모두 만족할 때만 `koDiagnostics.hot=true`가 된다.
213: - 기본값: `minSentences=4`, `minEojeols=20`, `spacing.eojeolLengthCV < 0.38`,
214:   `comma.perSentence < 1`, `posProxy.matchedCount >= 10`,
215:   `posProxy.classDiversity < 0.26`.
216: - 쉼표가 적다는 사실만으로 hot 처리하지 않는다. spacing과 suffix diversity가 동시에
217:   맞아야 한다.
218: - `.patina.default.yaml`의 `stylometry.ko_diagnostics.enabled=false`로 이 composite만 끌 수
219:   있다. 기존 burstiness/MATTR/lexicon 신호는 그대로 동작한다.
220: 
221: Calibration note (2026-05-22): `npm run benchmark:katfish-ko -- --write --basename katfish-ko-latest` evaluates this zero-dependency composite against a private KatFish checkout plus the 250-row public-web KO human-control set. Current aggregate result: KatFish catch rate improves from 58.9% without KO diagnostics to 74.8% with KO diagnostics (+15.9 pp), while the public-web human-control FP rate stays 42/250 (16.8%, +0 rows). The KatFish raw rows are not committed because the upstream repository does not expose repo-level license metadata; only aggregate reports are tracked.
222: 
223: ---
224: 
225: ## 6. Hot Decision Rule
226: 
227: 단락 수준 SUSPECT 판정은 단순한 OR 규칙으로 결정한다. v3.7 이후 lexicon density가
228: 세 번째 hot 신호로, ko diagnostic composite가 네 번째 축으로 추가되었고, #334/#391
229: 이후 discourse-tell 두 종류(fake-candor opener, decorative thematic break)가
230: 문서 수준 density gate를 통과했을 때 추가 disjunct로 합류했다(상세 calibration은 §16).
231: 
232: ```
233: paragraph is SUSPECT iff
234:   burstiness_band == "low"  OR  MATTR_band == "low"  OR
235:   (lexicon_density > threshold AND lexicon_min_hits is satisfied)  OR
236:   koDiagnostics.hot == true  OR
237:   (fakeCandor.doc_count >= 2 AND paragraph_candor_count >= 1)  OR
238:   (thematicBreaks.doc_count >= 3 AND paragraph_break_count >= 1)
239:   OR (ko: ending_monotony — declarative_da_ratio >= 0.6 AND declarative_da_count >= 2
240:           AND burstiness_cv < burstiness_band.low AND paragraph_tokens >= 20)
241: ```
242: 
243: ### 근거
244: 
245: - 네 신호 중 하나만 약해도 우선 검토할 만한 edit hotspot이 된다
246: - AND 조건은 recall 이 너무 낮아 v1 acceptance criteria(AI 시드 7/10)를 만족하기 어렵다
247: - false positive 는 `FalsePositiveControl` 평가(자연 시드 ≤2/10)로 견제한다
248: - ko/zh/ja lexicon은 단일 hit를 hot 판정으로 쓰지 않고 audit hint로만 남긴다. 짧은
249:   한국어 전문/정책 문단에서 `기반`, `흐름` 같은 보통 명사가 단독으로 터지는 오탐을
250:   막기 위해 CJK 기본 `lexicon_min_hits`는 2다.
251: - ko diagnostic signal은 내부에서 AND composite를 사용하므로 쉼표/조사 같은 단일 특징만으로는
252:   OR-rule에 들어오지 않는다
253: - ko ending-monotony signal(균일 평서형 -다)은 짧은 AI 한국어가 평탄한 한다체를 균일한
254:   문장 길이로 쓰는 경향을 잡는다. burstiness가 낮고(`cv < low`) 평서형 `-다` 종결이
255:   우세할 때(비율 ≥ 0.6, 2회 이상)만 켜지며, 같은 `-다`라도 문장 길이 변동이 큰 격식
256:   사람 한국어(cv 높음)와 대화체(요/습니다)는 배제된다. 3문장 미만이라 burstiness band
257:   gate가 비활성인 짧은 단락도 포괄하되, 초단문 오탐을 막으려 단락 토큰 ≥ 20을 요구한다.
258:   KO×GPT rebaseline에서 catch 45.0%→82.5%, KO recall 59.2%→70.8%(F1 0.644→0.716)로
259:   개선되고 사람 대조군 FP는 12.8%→14.0%(공개 CI 내)에 머문다. en/zh/ja에는 적용되지 않는다.
260: - discourse-tell disjunct(fake-candor / thematic-break)는 단독 단락 특징이 아니라
261:   문서 수준 density gate(candor opener ≥2, thematic break ≥3)를 먼저 통과해야 켜진다.
262:   gate 통과 후 해당 tell을 실제로 1회 이상 보유한 단락만 hot으로 합류하므로, 단일
263:   opener나 단일 divider가 문단을 단독으로 hot 처리하지 않는다(#334/#391).
264: 
265: ### 임계값 재조정
266: 
267: v1 결과에서 false positive 가 한도(자연 시드 2/10)를 넘기면, `.patina.default.yaml`의
268: `stylometry.burstiness.bands.low` 또는 `stylometry.ttr.bands.low` 를 보수적으로 낮춰
269: 재평가한다 (예: 0.30 → 0.27). lexicon 쪽은 `lexicon.density_threshold`로 조정한다.
270: ko composite는 `stylometry.ko_diagnostics.bands`에서 조정한다.
271: 
272: ---
273: 
274: ## 7. Sentence Zoom Rule
275: 
276: hot 단락 내부에서 문장 수준 sub-flag 을 생성한다. 단락 전체를 재작성 대상으로
277: 지정하지 않고, 가장 의심스러운 인접 문장 그룹만 가리키는 것이 목적이다.
278: 
279: ### 규칙
280: 
281: ```
282: similarity_threshold = 0.20   # ±20% token count
283: 
284: FOR each pair of adjacent sentences (S_i, S_{i+1}) in hot paragraph:
285:     smaller = min(len(S_i.tokens), len(S_{i+1}.tokens))
286:     larger  = max(len(S_i.tokens), len(S_{i+1}.tokens))
287:     IF smaller == 0:
288:         continue
289:     IF (larger - smaller) / smaller < similarity_threshold:
290:         mark (S_i, S_{i+1}) as adjacent-similar pair
291: 
292: merge overlapping pairs into contiguous groups
293: emit groups of length ≥ 2 as sub-flags
294: ```
295: 
296: - 기준은 토큰 수 차이 < 20% (i.e., 길이 비가 대략 1.0 ~ 1.20 범위)
297: - 단일 문장은 sub-flag 으로 emit 하지 않는다 (인접 유사 페어 필요)
298: - 임계값은 `stylometry.sentence_zoom.similarity_threshold` 에서 조정 가능
299: 
300: ### 출력
301: 
302: 각 sub-flag 은 `P{n}.S{m..k}` 범위로 표기한다. 예: `P2.S1-S3`.
303: 
304: ---
305: 
306: ## 8. Skip Conditions
307: 
308: 오버헤드 회피를 위해 짧은 텍스트는 통째로 skip 한다.
309: 
310: | 조건 | 동작 |
311: |------|------|
312: | 단락 수 ≤ 2 | 4.6단계 전체 skip — meta block 생략 |
313: | 전체 문장 수 ≤ 2 | 4.6단계 전체 skip — meta block 생략 |
314: | 단락 내 문장 수 < 3 | CV 값은 기록하지만 burstiness band/hot 판정은 생략 → MATTR/lexicon 만 평가 |
315: | 단락 내 토큰 수 = 0 | 해당 단락 skip |
316: | 언어가 `stylometry.languages` 에 없음 | 4.6단계 전체 skip (기본값은 ko/en/zh/ja 포함) |
317: 
318: 임계값은 4.5단계 의미 앵커 추출의 skip 조건과 동일하다.
319: 
320: > **참고:** 4.6단계가 skip 되면 5a·5b 입력에 `<suspect-zones>` meta block이 포함되지 않는다.
321: > 이 경우 파이프라인은 정상 진행한다 (suspect-zone 정보 없이).
322: 
323: ---
324: 
325: ## 9. LLM Delivery Format
326: 
327: 분석 결과는 5a 입력 직전에 텍스트 상단에 삽입되는 meta block 과, 본문 내 단락 prefix
328: 두 가지 형태로 LLM 에 전달된다.
329: 
330: ### Meta Block
331: 
332: ```
333: <suspect-zones lang="{ko|en}">
334: - P{n}: burstiness={float} ({low|mid|high}), MATTR={float} ({low|mid|high}) — {short reason}
335: - P{n}.S{m}: {short reason}
336: </suspect-zones>
337: ```
338: 
339: 규칙:
340: - hot 단락만 entry 로 emit (mid/high 단락은 생략)
341: - burstiness 와 MATTR 은 소수 둘째 자리까지 표기
342: - short reason 은 한국어 한 줄 (예: `문장 길이 균질`, `어휘 반복 다수`)
343: - 문장 sub-flag 은 별도 entry 로 추가
344: - meta block 전체는 사용자 출력에 노출하지 않는다 (anchor 와 동일 정책)
345: 
346: ### Body Prefix
347: 
348: hot 단락 본문 첫머리에 토큰 prefix `«P{n} SUSPECT»` 를 삽입한다.
349: 
350: ```
351: «P2 SUSPECT» 이 도구는 단순한 자동완성을 넘어선다. ...
352: ```
353: 
354: - 이 prefix 는 5a/5b 처리 시점에 LLM 이 즉시 인지하도록 신호를 reinforce 한다
355: - 5c 자기검수 단계에서 prefix 와 meta block 모두 제거된 최종 출력을 생성한다
356: - 사용자 대면 출력에는 prefix 가 절대 남지 않아야 한다
357: 
358: ### 5c 회귀 체크 (옵션)
359: 
360: 5c 자기검수 단계에서 hot zone 들이 모두 처리되었는지 확인할 수 있다.
361: - 모든 hot 단락이 어떤 형태로든 재작성되었는지 (단락 비교)
362: - 처리되지 않은 hot zone 이 있으면 경고 (강제 재처리는 v1 범위 밖)
363: 
364: ---
365: 
366: ## 10. Worked Example
367: 
368: ### 한국어 예시
369: 
370: 입력 (단락 1개):
371: 
372: ```
373: 이 도구는 단순한 자동완성을 넘어선다. 이 도구는 사용자의 의도를 이해한다.
374: 이 도구는 효율적인 협업을 가능하게 한다. 이 도구는 혁신적인 생산성을 제공한다.
375: 이 도구는 다양한 언어를 지원한다.
376: ```
377: 
378: ### Step-by-step
379: 
380: **Tokenize (어절 단위)**
381: 
382: | 문장 | 토큰 수 |
383: |------|---------|
384: | S1 | 5 |
385: | S2 | 5 |
386: | S3 | 5 |
387: | S4 | 5 |
388: | S5 | 5 |
389: 
390: **Burstiness**
391: 
392: ```
393: mean = 5
394: stddev = 0
395: CV = 0 / 5 = 0.00
396: band = low (< 0.30)
397: ```
398: 
399: **MATTR**
400: 
401: 전체 토큰 수 = 25 < 50 → simple TTR fallback.
402: 
403: ```
404: lower_tokens = [이, 도구는, 단순한, 자동완성을, 넘어선다, 이, 도구는, 사용자의,
405:                  의도를, 이해한다, 이, 도구는, 효율적인, 협업을, 가능하게, 한다,
406:                  이, 도구는, 혁신적인, 생산성을, 제공한다, 이, 도구는, 다양한,
407:                  언어를, 지원한다]
408:                  # 26 tokens
409: unique = {이, 도구는, 단순한, 자동완성을, 넘어선다, 사용자의, 의도를, 이해한다,
410:           효율적인, 협업을, 가능하게, 한다, 혁신적인, 생산성을, 제공한다,
411:           다양한, 언어를, 지원한다}
412:         # 18 unique
413: TTR = 18 / 26 ≈ 0.69
414: band = mid (0.55 ≤ 0.69 ≤ 0.70)
415: ```
416: 
417: **Hot Decision**
418: 
419: burstiness=low → **SUSPECT** (OR 조건 충족).
420: 
421: **Sentence Zoom**
422: 
423: 모든 인접 문장이 동일한 토큰 수(5) → 차이 0% < 20% → S1-S5 전체가 한 그룹.
424: 
425: **Meta Block 출력**
426: 
427: ```
428: <suspect-zones lang="ko">
429: - P1: burstiness=0.00 (low), MATTR=0.69 (mid) — 문장 길이 균질
430: - P1.S1-S5: 인접 문장 토큰 수 동일
431: </suspect-zones>
432: ```
433: 
434: **Body Prefix**
435: 
436: ```
437: «P1 SUSPECT» 이 도구는 단순한 자동완성을 넘어선다. 이 도구는 사용자의 의도를 이해한다.
438: 이 도구는 효율적인 협업을 가능하게 한다. 이 도구는 혁신적인 생산성을 제공한다.
439: 이 도구는 다양한 언어를 지원한다.
440: ```
441: 
442: ### 영어 예시
443: 
444: 입력 (단락 1개):
445: 
446: ```
447: The tool is innovative. The tool is efficient. The tool is reliable.
448: The tool is scalable. The tool is essential.
449: ```
450: 
451: **Tokenize (단어 단위)**
452: 
453: 각 문장 4 토큰. 전체 토큰 수 = 20.
454: 
455: **Burstiness**
456: 
457: ```
458: mean = 4, stddev = 0, CV = 0.00 → low
459: ```
460: 
461: **MATTR**
462: 
463: 전체 토큰 수 20 < 50 → simple TTR.
464: 
465: ```
466: lower_tokens = [the, tool, is, innovative, the, tool, is, efficient, the, tool,
467:                 is, reliable, the, tool, is, scalable, the, tool, is, essential]
468: unique = {the, tool, is, innovative, efficient, reliable, scalable, essential}
469:         # 8 unique
470: TTR = 8 / 20 = 0.40
471: band = low (< 0.55)
472: ```
473: 
474: **Hot Decision**
475: 
476: burstiness=low AND MATTR=low → **SUSPECT** (둘 다 충족).
477: 
478: **Meta Block 출력**
479: 
480: ```
481: <suspect-zones lang="en">
482: - P1: burstiness=0.00 (low), MATTR=0.40 (low) — uniform sentence length, low lexical diversity
483: - P1.S1-S5: identical token counts
484: </suspect-zones>
485: ```
486: 
487: **Body Prefix**
488: 
489: ```
490: «P1 SUSPECT» The tool is innovative. The tool is efficient. The tool is reliable.
491: The tool is scalable. The tool is essential.
492: ```
493: 
494: ---
495: 
496: ## 11. Roadmap (v2+)
497: 
498: 현재 기본 경로에서 의도적으로 제외했거나 ship 이후 후속 검토만 남은 항목.
499: 
500: **zh/ja decision:** 기본 경로는 char-token fallback으로 확정한다. go 조건은 "무의존성,
501: Node 18 호환, 문장 길이 CV/MATTR가 공백 없는 zh/ja 문장에서 0-token으로 붕괴하지 않음"이다.
502: jieba/sudachi/mecab 같은 형태소 의존성은 no-go다. go 조건을 다시 열려면 (1) pure JS 또는
503: optional dependency로 설치 실패가 없는 경로, (2) ko/en/zh/ja benchmark에서 false positive
504: 증가가 허용 범위 내라는 근거, (3) 패키지 크기/속도 회귀가 문서화되어야 한다.
505: 
506: | 항목 | 설명 | 도입 후보 시점 |
507: |------|------|----------------|
508: | ~~n-gram redundancy~~ | ~~bi/trigram 반복도~~ — **dropped, §15 negative finding** | — |
509: | ~~AI-lexicon overlap~~ | ~~28-패턴 외 AI 특유 어구 사전 매칭~~ — **shipped v3.7, §16** | v3.7 |
510: | ~~zh/ja character fallback~~ | ~~Han/Kana character-token burstiness/TTR~~ — **shipped for 4.6** | current |
511: | ~~ko diagnostic composite~~ | ~~spacing/comma/suffix proxy~~ — **shipped as conservative AND signal, §5.1** | current |
512: | Perplexity proxy | small LM 또는 cloze prompt 기반 token-level surprise | v4 후보 |
513: | Broader function-word distribution | en/zh/ja 포함 기능어 빈도 분포 차이 | v4 후보 |
514: | GPTZero / Originality 연동 | 외부 detector API 결과를 hot 신호로 합산 | v4+ 후보 |
515: | 형태소 분석 기반 ko 토큰화 | 어절 → 형태소 단위로 정밀화 | v2+ |
516: | zh/ja 형태소 분석 통합 | 단어 경계 처리; 위 go/no-go 충족 시에만 재검토 | v4+ 후보 |
517: | 사용자 idiolect 학습 | 개인 작성 스타일 학습 후 false positive 억제 | 별도 트랙 |
518: | 정량 라벨 데이터셋 | 언어별 50+ 라벨 데이터 기반 임계값 튜닝 | v1 결과 후 검토 |
519: 
520: ---
521: 
522: ## 12. Known Limitations
523: 
524: - **Korean morphology coarseness**: 어절 단위 토큰화는 morpheme-level 분석과 다르다.
525:   같은 명사의 조사 변형(`도구는`, `도구가`, `도구를`)을 서로 다른 token 으로 취급하므로
526:   MATTR 이 실제보다 약간 높게 나올 수 있다.
527: - **Korean diagnostic composite is conservative**: 쉼표 없음 + 균질한 어절 길이 + 낮은 suffix
528:   class diversity가 모두 맞아야 하므로 recall보다는 false-positive 억제를 우선한다. 실제
529:   KatFish/2025+ corpus 재보정 전에는 넓은 성능 주장으로 쓰지 않는다.
530: - **Short text noise**: 단락 ≤2 또는 문장 ≤2 인 텍스트는 통계적으로 신뢰할 수 없어 skip
531:   한다. 이 경우 4.6단계 전체가 비활성화된다.
532: - **Window=50 fallback**: MATTR window 보다 짧은 단락은 simple TTR 로 fallback 하므로
533:   길이 의존 편향이 일부 남는다. 단락 비교 시 길이 차가 크면 신중히 해석한다.
534: - **LLM non-determinism**: 통계 계산 자체는 결정론적이나, 5a/5b 가 hot 정보를 어떻게
535:   활용할지는 LLM 의 판단에 달려 있다. acceptance criteria 는 hot 표시 정확도까지만 보장한다.
536: - **No external detector**: v1 은 외부 detector API(GPTZero 등)와 통합하지 않는다.
537:   외부 신호는 v3 후보 로드맵 항목이다.
538: - **False split**: 약어(`Mr.`, `e.g.`) 와 소수점(`3.14`) 같은 종결부호 오인식을 v1 에서
539:   감수한다. 시드 평가에서 false positive 비율이 한도를 넘기면 v1.1 에서 보강한다.
540: - **Language scope**: 4.6 stylometry는 ko/en/zh/ja를 지원한다. zh/ja는 형태소가 아니라
541:   character-token fallback이므로 MATTR 해석은 보수적으로만 사용한다. 4.7 lexicon도
542:   en/ko/zh/ja 기본 사전을 제공한다.
543: 
544: ---
545: 
546: ## 13. Calibration Appendix (v3.5.1)
547: 
548: v3.5.0 자체 시드 평가는 만점이었으나(AI 10/10, 자연 0/10), 외부 데이터에서는 신호가 약했다. 시드 픽스처를 작성한 측과 측정 알고리즘이 같았기 때문이다(자기 일관성). 이 절은 v3.5.1 calibration 의 근거 데이터다.
549: 
550: ### 외부 측정 (300 단락)
551: 
552: | Source | n | hot rate (v3.5.0, 0.25) | hot rate (v3.5.1, 0.30) | CV median |
553: |--------|---|--------------------------|--------------------------|-----------|
554: | HC3 ChatGPT 답변 (en) | 100 | 57.0% | 66.0% | 0.220 |
555: | HC3 human 답변 (en) | 100 | 8.0% | 12.0% | 0.511 |
556: | Wikipedia 도입 단락 (en) | 100 | 19.0% | 23.0% | 0.386 |
557: 
558: ### 임계값 sweep 핵심 결과
559: 
560: burstiness × MATTR 35조합을 모두 측정. **AI ≥70% AND max FP ≤20% 동시 만족하는 조합은 0개.** Pareto frontier 상위:
561: 
562: | burst | mattr | AI hot | HC3 FP | Wiki FP | utility |
563: |-------|-------|--------|--------|---------|---------|
564: | 0.25 | 0.55 (v3.5.0) | 57% | 8% | 19% | 38 |
565: | **0.30** | **0.55 (v3.5.1)** | **66%** | **12%** | **23%** | **43** |
566: | 0.35 | 0.55 | 73% | 22% | 34% | 39 |
567: | 0.40 | 0.55 | 84% | 35% | 57% | 27 |
568: 
569: v3.5.1 은 utility 최대점. 0.35 이상은 Wikipedia FP 가 급증해 백과사전체 자연 텍스트를 과도하게 hot 으로 표시.
570: 
571: ### MATTR 의 약한 변별력 — 정직한 한계
572: 
573: MATTR 임계값을 `0.55 → 0.78` 로 광범위하게 sweep 해도 AI 와 사람의 발화 빈도 차이가 거의 없었다.
574: 
575: | MATTR threshold | AI MATTR-only fires | Human MATTR-only fires |
576: |-----------------|---------------------|------------------------|
577: | 0.65 | 2/100 | 3/100 |
578: | 0.70 | 6/100 | 5/100 |
579: | 0.75 | 16/100 | 19/100 |
580: 
581: MATTR 0.75 에서는 사람 텍스트가 오히려 더 자주 hot 발화. 자유 산문에서는 어휘 다양성이 AI/사람 변별 신호로 약하다는 의미. v3.5.1 은 **MATTR 임계값을 0.55 로 유지**한다 (실질적으로 거의 발화하지 않는 안전 신호로 보존; 임계값 상향은 false positive 만 늘림). 후속 §15 에서 n-gram 반복도도 같은 한계를 보임 — 단순 redundancy 신호 추가만으로는 Pareto 벽을 못 뚫는다.
582: 
583: ### Wikipedia 의 register 문제
584: 
585: Wikipedia 도입 단락은 백과사전체 register 라 자연스럽게 균질한 문장 길이를 가진다(CV median 0.386). v3.5.1 에서 23% false positive 는 v1 acceptance(≤20%) 를 살짝 위반하나 register issue 에서 비롯된 구조적 한계로 본다. 단순 임계값 조정으로는 해소되지 않으며, §15 에서 확인했듯 n-gram redundancy 도 도움이 되지 않는다 — 백과사전체 균질성과 AI 균질성을 분리하려면 perplexity 또는 AI-lexicon 같은 다른 축의 신호가 필요하다.
586: 
587: ### 운용상 권고
588: 
589: v3.5.1 은 advisory marker 로 사용하라. 패턴 카탈로그가 명명한 28 개 신호의 **보조 입력**이며, 단독 결정 신호로 쓰기엔 catch rate(66%) 가 부족하다. 단락이 hot 표시되면 LLM 이 우선 검토하고, 표시되지 않더라도 패턴 단계가 정상 작동한다.
590: 
591: ### 외부 검증 재현
592: 
593: `.omc/research/eval_external_v2.py` 로 측정, `.omc/research/threshold_sweep.py` 로 sweep 수행. raw 결과는 `.omc/research/external_results_v2.json`. 재실행 시 HuggingFace `Hello-SimpleAI/HC3` + `wikimedia/wikipedia` 20231101.en 다운로드 발생.
594: 
595: ---
596: 
597: ## 14. Korean External Validation (post-v3.5.1)
598: 
599: v3.5.1 calibration 은 영어 데이터(HC3 + Wikipedia)만 사용했다. 이후 NamuWiki 100 단락 (`heegyu/namuwiki`, CC-BY-NC-SA 2.0 KR) 으로 한국어 자연 텍스트 검증을 수행했다.
600: 
601: ### 결과
602: 
603: | Source | n | CV median | MATTR median | hot rate (v3.5.1) |
604: |--------|---|-----------|--------------|-------------------|
605: | NamuWiki (ko 자연) | 100 | 0.592 | 0.941 | **11.0%** |
606: | HC3 human (en 자연) | 100 | 0.511 | 0.792 | 12.0% |
607: | Wikipedia (en 자연) | 100 | 0.386 | 0.770 | 23.0% |
608: 
609: NamuWiki 한국어 단락의 burstiness CV 중앙값(0.592)은 영어 자연 텍스트보다 더 높고, hot rate 11% 는 v1 자연 대조 한도(≤20%) 를 여유롭게 통과한다. **v3.5.1 임계값이 한국어에서도 그대로 유효함을 확인.**
610: 
611: ### MATTR 의 한국어 특이성
612: 
613: MATTR median 0.941 — 영어 자연 텍스트(0.77~0.79) 보다 훨씬 높다. 이유: 어절 단위 토큰화가 `도구는`/`도구가`/`도구를` 같은 조사 변형을 별개 토큰으로 취급해 어휘 다양성이 인위적으로 부풀려진다. §12 Known Limitations 에서 명시한 한계가 데이터로 확인됨. 한국어에서 MATTR 신호는 사실상 발화하지 않는다 (NamuWiki 100 단락 중 0건 hot).
614: 
615: ### 한국어 AI 텍스트 측정 (post-v3.7.0)
616: 
617: 자유 배포 한국어 AI 코퍼스가 없어 NamuWiki 100 토픽을 시드로 Claude (claude -p, opus) 에 paired 한국어 단락 100건을 자가 생성한 뒤 측정했다 (`.omc/research/ko_ai_robust.py`).
618: 
619: | 지표 | ko/AI (Claude 생성, n=100) | ko/human (NamuWiki, n=100) | gap |
620: |------|---------------------------|----------------------------|-----|
621: | CV median | 0.200 | 0.592 | — |
622: | MATTR median | 0.982 | 0.941 | — |
623: | lexicon density median | 0.00 | n/a | — |
624: | **hot rate (v3.5.1: burst+MATTR)** | **82.0%** | 11.0% | **+71pp** |
625: | **hot rate (v3.7.0: 3-signal OR)** | **83.0%** | 13.0% | **+70pp** |
626: 
627: **해석:**
628: - 한국어 AI catch rate (82~83%) 가 영어 AI catch rate (76%, HC3) 보다 높다. Claude 가 생성한 한국어 백과사전체 산문이 균질한 문장 길이를 더 강하게 보이는 경향.
629: - v3.5.1 burstiness 임계값이 한국어에서 그대로 잘 작동 — 별도 calibration 불필요.
630: - MATTR 은 한국어에서 0/100 발화 (median 0.982). 어절 토큰화 한계로 사실상 죽은 신호.
631: - lexicon 은 +1pp 보조 (9/100 발화). 영어 (+10pp) 보다 약함 — Korean lexicon (90 entries) 의 효과는 제한적이며, 이후 사용자 corpus 측정으로 재조정 여지 있음.
632: - ko/human FP (13%) vs ko/AI hot (83%) 의 gap 70pp 는 영어 gap (52pp) 보다도 크다. 한국어에서 stylometric 신호가 강하게 동작.
633: 
634: **한계:**
635: - AI 측 데이터가 Claude 자가 생성이라 OOM (out-of-our-model) AI 시스템(GPT-4, Gemini, Llama 등) 검증은 별도. v4 에서 멀티모델 paired corpus 로 확장 예정.
636: - NamuWiki 토픽이 알파벳 가나다순 첫 100건이라 도메인 편향 가능성 (라이트노벨 캐릭터, 가면라이더 시리즈가 다소 많음). 도메인 stratified sample 은 v4 후보.
637: 
638: ---
639: 
640: ## 15. v3.6 n-gram Repetition — Negative Finding
641: 
642: §13 Pareto 벽을 뚫기 위한 v2 후보였던 n-gram 반복도 신호를 동일 코퍼스(400 단락 = HC3 AI/human + Wikipedia + NamuWiki) 에 추가 측정했다.
643: 
644: ### 측정값 분포
645: 
646: | Source | bigram redundancy median | trigram redundancy median |
647: |--------|--------------------------|---------------------------|
648: | HC3 ChatGPT | 0.051 | 0.000 |
649: | HC3 human | **0.071** | 0.012 |
650: | Wikipedia | 0.046 | 0.000 |
651: | NamuWiki | 0.000 | 0.000 |
652: 
653: ### 핵심 발견
654: 
655: **HC3 human 의 bigram redundancy (0.071) 가 HC3 AI (0.051) 보다 오히려 높다.** Q&A 형식의 사람 답변이 ChatGPT 답변보다 구조적으로 더 반복적이라는 의미. n-gram 반복도는 AI/사람 변별 신호로 쓸모가 없다 — 적어도 단순 redundancy 정의(`1 - unique/total`) 로는 그렇다.
656: 
657: trigram 임계값 sweep 에서도 AI catch 와 human FP 가 거의 같은 비율로 함께 오르내림 (gap 변동 ±2pp). 신호 추가 효과 없음.
658: 
659: ### 운용 결정
660: 
661: n-gram 반복도를 ship 하지 않는다. v3.5.1 상태 유지. roadmap 에서 n-gram 항목을 제거하고, 다음 후보로 이동:
662: 
663: - **Perplexity proxy** (token-level surprise) — 구조적 균질성과 다른 축의 신호. AI 텍스트는 token 별 conditional probability 가 일관되게 높음 (LM 이 다음 토큰을 잘 예측). 구현: small LM (예: GPT-2 small) 또는 cloze prompt 기반 추정. v4 후보.
664: - **AI-lexicon overlap** — 28-패턴이 명명하지 못한 AI 특유 어구 사전("transformative", "cutting-edge", "leveraging" 류) 와의 단순 매칭. 패턴 카탈로그의 통계적 보강. v3.7 후보.
665: - **Function-word distribution** — AI 와 사람의 of/in/the/는/이/를 등 기능어 빈도 차이. Stylometric authorship attribution 문헌 (Mosteller & Wallace 1964 연속) 의 고전 신호. v4 후보.
666: 
667: ### 재현
668: 
669: `.omc/research/v3_6_ngram_probe.py` 로 측정, raw 결과는 `.omc/research/v3_6_results.json`. HuggingFace `Hello-SimpleAI/HC3` + `wikimedia/wikipedia` + `heegyu/namuwiki` 다운로드 발생.
670: 
671: ---
672: 
673: ## 16. AI-Lexicon Overlap Signal (v3.7)
674: 
675: §13 calibration 과 §15 n-gram 부정 결과 모두 같은 결론을 가리켰다 — 구조적 균질성(burstiness, MATTR, n-gram redundancy)은 백과사전체와 AI를 충분히 분리하지 못한다. 다른 축의 신호가 필요했다. v3.7은 28-패턴 카탈로그가 명시적으로 잡지 못하는 AI 특유 어구를 평면 사전(flat dictionary)으로 추가 매칭해 어휘 축의 신호를 도입한다.
676: 
677: ### 알고리즘
678: 
679: ```
680: For paragraph P with tokens T:
681:   matches = number of lexicon entries that appear in P
682:             (case-insensitive, whole-word for "Strict matches",
683:              substring for "Multi-word phrases")
684:   density = matches / len(T) * 1000   # matches per 1000 tokens
685: 
686:   hot iff density > threshold AND min_hot_matches is satisfied
687: ```
688: 
689: 기본 threshold = `3.0` (1,000 토큰당 3회). `lexicon.density_threshold`로 설정 가능.
690: 기본 `min_hot_matches`는 영어 1, 한국어/중국어/일본어 2다. CJK 단일 lexicon hit는
691: audit hint로 표시하지만 단락을 hot으로 만들지는 않는다.
692: 
693: ### 단락 hot 결정 규칙 (v3.7 당시 3-signal OR)
694: 
695: ```
696: paragraph is SUSPECT iff
697:   burstiness_band == "low" OR MATTR_band == "low" OR
698:   (lexicon_density > threshold AND min_hot_matches is satisfied)
699: ```
700: 
701: v3.7에서는 §6의 2-signal OR 규칙을 3-signal OR로 확장했다. burstiness/MATTR는 분포적 신호, lexicon은 어휘적 신호 — 다른 축이라 OR 결합 시 둘 다 합산 효과를 냈다. 이후 ko diagnostic composite가 네 번째 축으로, #334/#391의 discourse-tell 두 종류가 문서 수준 density gate를 전제로 한 disjunct로 합류했다. 따라서 현재 전체 규칙은 §6의 4-signal OR에 discourse-tell 두 disjunct를 더한 형태다(stylometry 분포 신호 + lexicon 어휘 신호 + ko composite + discourse tell).
702: 
703: ### 사전 파일
704: 
705: - `lexicon/ai-en.md` — 영어 43개 strict + 45개 phrase = 88 entries
706: - `lexicon/ai-ko.md` — 한국어 v2.0.0, 16개 strict + 17개 phrase = 33 entries
707: - `lexicon/ai-zh.md` — 중국어 60개 phrase = 60 entries
708: - `lexicon/ai-ja.md` — 일본어 60개 phrase = 60 entries
709: 
710: 탐색은 `Glob lexicon/ai-{lang}.md`로 자동. 기본 `lexicon.languages`는
711: `[en, ko, zh, ja]`이다. 사용자가 `custom/lexicon/ai-{lang}.md` 를 두면 우선 로드.
712: 
713: ### 매칭 정책
714: 
715: - **Strict matches** (Markdown `## Strict matches` 섹션): 대소문자 무시 whole-word 매칭. CJK(ko/zh/ja)는 substring으로 근사 — 한국어 어절과 zh/ja character fallback에서 multi-character entry가 통째 token으로 남지 않기 때문이다
716: - **Multi-word phrases** (Markdown `## Multi-word phrases` 섹션): 대소문자 무시 substring 매칭. `~` 가 포함된 phrase(예: `~의 지평을 넓히다`, `不仅~而且`, `~と言えるでしょう`)는 `~` 을 wildcard 로 취급 (`.{0,40}`)
717: - 한 paragraph 안에서 같은 entry 가 여러 번 나와도 1로 계산 (entry 단위 카운트)
718: 
719: ### LLM 전달 형식 확장
720: 
721: 기존 `<suspect-zones>` meta block을 lexicon hit 정보로 확장한다.
722: 
723: ```
724: <suspect-zones lang="ko">
725: - P1: burstiness=0.18 (low) — 문장 길이 균질
726: - P2: lexicon_density=4.2/1000 — AI-lexicon hits: "혁신적인 접근, 패러다임 전환, 시너지"
727: - P3: burstiness=0.22 (low), lexicon_density=3.1/1000 — 균질 + AI 어휘 다수
728: </suspect-zones>
729: ```
730: 
731: 규칙:
732: - lexicon hit이 있는 단락은 매칭된 entry 중 최대 5개를 짧은 인용으로 표기
733: - burstiness/MATTR hot 과 lexicon hot 이 동시 발화하면 한 줄에 합쳐서 표기
734: - meta block 전체는 사용자 출력에 노출하지 않는다 (§9 Anchor 정책과 동일)
735: 
736: ### Body Prefix
737: 
738: §9의 `«P{n} SUSPECT»` prefix 는 lexicon-only hot 단락에도 동일하게 적용한다. LLM 입력에는 hot의 발화 사유가 무엇이든 동일한 신호로 도착한다.
739: 
740: ### v3.7 Calibration (외부 400 단락)
741: 
742: 같은 코퍼스(HC3 ChatGPT/human + Wikipedia + NamuWiki)로 `density_threshold` 0.5–5.0 범위 sweep.
743: 
744: | Source | n | v3.5.1 hot | v3.7 hot (thr=2.0) |
745: |--------|---|------------|---------------------|
746: | HC3 ChatGPT (en AI) | 100 | **66.0%** | **76.0%** |
747: | HC3 human (en) | 100 | 12.0% | 19.0% |
748: | Wikipedia (en) | 100 | 23.0% | **25.0%** |
749: | NamuWiki (ko) | 100 | 11.0% | 13.0% |
750: 
751: Pareto frontier (3-signal OR, threshold sweep):
752: 
753: | lex_thr | AI% | HC3% | Wiki% | Namu% | utility |
754: |---------|-----|------|-------|-------|---------|
755: | 0.5–2.5 (plateau) | 76.0 | 19.0 | 25.0 | 13.0 | +51 |
756: | 3.0 | 76.0 | 17.0 | 25.0 | 13.0 | +51 |
757: | 5.0 | 76.0 | 15.0 | 25.0 | 13.0 | +51 |
758: | v3.5.1 baseline | 66.0 | 12.0 | 23.0 | 11.0 | +43 |
759: 
760: ### Acceptance criteria — 충족
761: 
762: | 기준 | 목표 | 실측 |
763: |------|------|------|
764: | AI catch (HC3 ChatGPT) | ≥ 75% | **76.0%** ✓ |
765: | max human FP | ≤ 25% | **25.0%** ✓ (Wikipedia, 경계) |
766: | NamuWiki 회귀 | v3.5.1 +5pp 이내 | 11% → 13% (+2pp) ✓ |
767: 
768: 3개 기준 모두 충족 → v3.7 ship 결정.
769: 
770: ### Threshold 선택 근거
771: 
772: `density_threshold = 3.0` 채택. v3.5.1 calibration 코퍼스(HC3/Wikipedia/NamuWiki)에서는 0.5–5.0 구간이 동일 catch/FP plateau였으나, 2026-06 modern-model 재기준(rb26 KO/EN measure-only 코퍼스)에서 영어 자연 대조군 오탐이 threshold 2.0에서 15%, 3.0에서 5%로 떨어지고 영어 AI 재현율(86.9%)·한국어 재현율은 불변이며 49-fixture 벤치마크가 100%/ROC·PR-AUC 1.000을 유지하므로 3.0을 채택한다. 운용 의미: "1,000 토큰당 AI lexicon entry 가 3개 초과로 나타나고, 언어별 최소 hit 수를 만족하면 단락 의심". 한국어 오탐은 burstiness 신호가 주도하므로 별도 보정 delta로 분리한다. 2026-05 Korean 25-row register pilot 이후 CJK 단일 hit는 hot에서 audit hint로 낮췄다.
773: 
774: ### Calibration drop list
775: 
776: 초기 lexicon 후보 중 다음 entry 들은 sweep 결과 사람 텍스트(특히 Wikipedia 백과사전체) 발화율이 AI 발화율과 같거나 높아 제외했다.
777: 
778: - Strict (drop): `intersection`, `principles`, `mindset`, `iterative`, `responsible`, `methodologies`, `redefine`, `accessible`, `equitable`
779: - Phrases (drop): `one of the most`, `in conjunction with`, `the power of`
780: 
781: 이 entry 들은 학술/전문 prose 에서 자연스럽게 등장하는 단어로, AI 의 promotional 어휘가 아니라 register 의 일부였다. 재추가 시 반드시 `.omc/research/v3_7_lexicon_eval.py` 로 회귀 측정.
782: 
783: ### 28-패턴 카탈로그와의 분리
784: 
785: lexicon 은 다음 카탈로그 항목과 **중복되지 않도록** 큐레이션됐다:
786: 
787: - `en-language.md` Pattern 7 (delve, tapestry, multifaceted, leverage 등 30개) — 카탈로그가 이미 다룸
788: - `en-content.md` Pattern 1, 4 (groundbreaking, transformative — paradigm shift 등 promotional 형용사) — 카탈로그가 이미 다룸
789: - `ko-language.md` Pattern 7, 8 (다양한, 활발한, 혁신적인, ~적 접미사 등) — 카탈로그가 이미 다룸
790: - `ko-style.md` Pattern 13, 18 (이를 통해, 도모하다, 본 사업 등) — 카탈로그가 이미 다룸
791: 
792: lexicon 의 en 88 / ko 96 / zh 60 / ja 60 entry 는 위 패턴들이 명시적으로 잡지 않는 영역(modal scaffolding, 추상명사, 의례적 도입/마무리 phrase) 만 추렸다.
793: 
794: ### zh/ja lexicon 스타터 팩 (v3.11.x)
795: 
796: `lexicon/ai-zh.md`와 `lexicon/ai-ja.md`는 각각 60개 phrase-only entry로 시작한다.
797: 두 언어는 whitespace token이 안정적이지 않으므로 모든 기본 entry를 `Multi-word phrases`
798: 섹션에 두고 substring/wildcard 매칭으로 처리한다. 회귀 fixture에는 lexicon-only hot
799: 샘플과 natural cold counterexample을 각 언어별로 추가했으며, `npm run benchmark` 기준
800: zh/ja 모두 false positive 0/4, AI catch 4/4를 유지한다. 더 큰 외부 corpus로 확장할 때는
801: 각 파일의 counterexample table을 먼저 검토하고, 백과사전·뉴스 register에서 비슷하게
802: 발화하는 entry는 drop한다.
803: 
804: ### 한국어 lexicon 의 검증 결과 + v3.8.0 재큐레이션
805: 
806: **v3.7.0 (90 entries) 측정**: ko/AI 100 단락(Claude 자가 생성) 에서 한국어 lexicon 발화는 9/100. burstiness 신호(82/100) 위에 +1 단락만 추가. 영어 lexicon (HC3 ChatGPT 에서 +10pp) 대비 한국어 효과 미미.
807: 
808: **v3.8.0 (102 entries) 재큐레이션**:
809: 
810: ko/AI 코퍼스 vs NamuWiki human 차별 빈도 마이닝(`.omc/research/v3_8_ko_lexicon_mine.py`) 으로 AI 측 doc-freq ≥4×, ratio ≥4.0 인 phrase 발굴. 도메인 아티팩트(`가면라이더`, `한국`, `시리즈` 등) 제외하고 register marker 12개 추가:
811: 
812: - Strict (8개): `평가된다`, `꼽힌다`, `가리킨다`, `사례로`, `다수의`, `알려져`, `일컬어진다`, `평가받다`
813: - Phrases (4개): `가운데 하나로`, `자리 잡았다`, `알려져 있다`, `~의 사례로`
814: 
815: **v3.12.x false-positive pruning (96 entries)**:
816: `환경`, `기반`, `흐름`, `측면`, `토대`, `가리킨다`는 기술 문서의 구체적 용례(`환경 변수`, `이벤트 기반`, `인증 흐름` 등)에서 짧은 단락을 과열시키는 bare strict entry라 기본 lexicon에서 제외했다. 같은 register 과잉은 더 구체적인 phrase나 retained marker(`자리매김`, `중요한 의미`, `생태계`, `양상` 등)로 잡는다.
817: 
818: **v3.8.0 결과** (동일 코퍼스 재측정, `.omc/research/v3_8_remeasure.py`):
819: 
820: | Source | n | v3.7.0 hot | v3.8.0 hot | Δ | lex fires |
821: |--------|---|-----------:|-----------:|---:|---------:|
822: | ko_ai (Claude) | 100 | 83% | **91%** | **+8pp** | 9 → 52 |
823: | namu_human FP | 100 | 13% | **13%** | **0pp** | 0 → 2 |
824: | hc3_ai (en) | 100 | 76% | 76% | — | (en lex unchanged) |
825: | hc3_human FP | 100 | 19% | 19% | — | — |
826: | wiki_human FP | 100 | 25% | 25% | — | — |
827: 
828: **Clean Pareto 개선** — Korean human FP 0pp regression, AI catch +8pp. 신규 entry 가 AI 측에서 강하게 (52/100 fires) 사람 측에서 약하게 (2/100 fires) 발화. 한국어 catch rate 91% 는 시스템 전체에서 가장 강한 신호.
829: 
830: **근본 원인 — 왜 v3.7.0 한국어 lexicon 은 약했는가**:
831: 
832: v3.7.0 lexicon 큐레이션은 저자(Track B)의 한국어 AI 직관에 의존했다. 실제 Claude 출력은 그 직관과 어휘 분포가 달랐다(`시너지 효과`, `패러다임의 전환` 같은 비즈니스 외래어를 별로 안 씀; 대신 백과사전체 passive 종결 `평가된다`, `가리킨다`, `꼽힌다` 를 자주 씀). v3.8.0 마이닝은 직관 대신 paired corpus 의 통계적 차별로 발굴 — 더 실증적.
833: 
834: **v3.8 운용 권고**: 한국어에서 lexicon 이 burstiness 와 함께 의미있는 보조 신호로 작동하기 시작했다(91%). 영어 측은 v3.7.0 시점의 직관 큐레이션이 그대로 잘 작동(+10pp 기여). 영어 lexicon 도 향후 paired corpus 마이닝으로 재검토 가능 — v4 후보.
835: 
836: ### 운용상 권고
837: 
838: v3.7 도 advisory marker 다. 28-패턴 카탈로그의 보조 입력이며, 단독 결정 신호로 쓰기엔 catch rate(76%) 가 여전히 부족하다. 단락이 hot 표시되면 LLM 이 우선 검토하고, 표시되지 않더라도 패턴 단계가 정상 작동한다. v3.5.1 운용 권고와 동일.
839: 
840: ### 재현
841: 
842: `.omc/research/v3_7_lexicon_eval.py` 로 측정. raw 결과는 `.omc/research/v3_7_results.json` (paragraph-level: text + cv + mattr + lex_density + lex_hits). 재실행 시 HuggingFace `Hello-SimpleAI/HC3` + `wikimedia/wikipedia` 20231101.en + `heegyu/namuwiki` 다운로드 발생.
````

## File: lexicon/ai-en.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: pack: ai-lexicon-en
  3: language: en
  4: version: 1.0.0
  5: entries: 88
  6: entry-provenance: lexicon/provenance/ai-en.json
  7: corpus-snapshot:
  8:   id: hape-en-gpt4o-vs-human-2026-05-22
  9:   status: current
 10:   source: HAP-E English paired corpus, GPT-4o 2024-08-06 continuations vs human chunk_2; see docs/benchmarks/lexicon-freshness-en-2026-05-22.md
 11:   last_validated: 2026-05-22
 12: ---
 13: 
 14: # AI-favored vocabulary (English)
 15: 
 16: Phrases that AI assistants reach for far more often than human writers, but
 17: which the 28-pattern catalog does not already enumerate. These extend the
 18: language/content/style packs without overlapping them.
 19: 
 20: The catalog already lists 30 vocabulary words in `en-language.md` Pattern 7
 21: (delve, tapestry, multifaceted, leverage, etc.) and the promotional/emphasis
 22: adjectives in `en-content.md` Patterns 1 and 4. This lexicon adds words and
 23: phrases beyond those — modal scaffolding, register tells, and corporate
 24: abstractions that the catalog has not yet named.
 25: 
 26: Match policy:
 27: - Strict matches use case-insensitive whole-word match
 28: - Multi-word phrases use case-insensitive substring match
 29: - A trailing `s` or `ing` form counts as the same entry; do not double-count
 30: 
 31: ## Strict matches (case-insensitive whole word)
 32: 
 33: - transformative
 34: - cutting-edge
 35: - bespoke
 36: - curated
 37: - dynamic
 38: - vibrant
 39: - seamless
 40: - seamlessly
 41: - streamline
 42: - streamlined
 43: - empower
 44: - empowering
 45: - enabling
 46: - align
 47: - alignment
 48: - pivot
 49: - ecosystem
 50: - skillset
 51: - toolkit
 52: - modalities
 53: - harness
 54: - unlock
 55: - bolster
 56: - amplify
 57: - accelerate
 58: - catalyst
 59: - inflection
 60: - meaningful
 61: - impactful
 62: - actionable
 63: - scalable
 64: - sustainable
 65: - inclusive
 66: - ethical
 67: - thoughtful
 68: - compelling
 69: - thrive
 70: - thriving
 71: - elevate
 72: - reimagine
 73: - rethink
 74: - envision
 75: - prioritize
 76: 
 77: ## Multi-word phrases (case-insensitive substring)
 78: 
 79: - a wide array of
 80: - a plethora of
 81: - a myriad of
 82: - in today's
 83: - in the modern era
 84: - in the digital age
 85: - ever-evolving
 86: - ever-changing
 87: - rapidly evolving
 88: - rapidly changing
 89: - fast-paced
 90: - the world of
 91: - the realm of
 92: - the landscape of
 93: - the future of
 94: - unlock the potential
 95: - realize the potential
 96: - harness the power
 97: - pave the way
 98: - usher in
 99: - a new era
100: - a new chapter
101: - a new frontier
102: - gain valuable insights
103: - glean insights
104: - valuable insights
105: - key insights
106: - key takeaways
107: - play a crucial role
108: - plays a vital role
109: - pave the path
110: - bridge the gap
111: - at the forefront
112: - at the heart of
113: - at its core
114: - holistic approach
115: - comprehensive approach
116: - best practices
117: - continuous improvement
118: - a deeper dive
119: - the bigger picture
120: - a robust framework
121: - the digital landscape
122: - the regulatory landscape
123: - the competitive landscape
124: 
125: ## Notes on each entry (why AI-favored)
126: 
127: Single words above cluster around three habits the catalog under-covers:
128: modal scaffolding ("empower", "harness", "unlock"), abstraction
129: nouns AI defaults to over concrete ones ("ecosystem", "toolkit",
130: "modalities"), and self-flattering quality
131: adjectives ("meaningful", "impactful", "thoughtful", "compelling"). None
132: duplicate `en-language.md` Pattern 7's word list — they extend it.
133: 
134: Calibration drop list (v3.7 eval, see core/stylometry.md §16):
135: "intersection", "principles", "mindset", "iterative", "responsible",
136: "methodologies", "redefine", "accessible", "equitable", "one of the most",
137: "in conjunction with", "the power of" — fired more on Wikipedia/HC3 human
138: than on HC3 ChatGPT. Do not re-add without re-running the eval.
139: 
140: Freshness drop list (2026-05-22 HAP-E re-mine, see
141: `docs/benchmarks/lexicon-freshness-en-2026-05-22.md`):
142: "state-of-the-art", "enable", "workflow", "framework", "dimensions",
143: "unleash", "elevated", "a wide range of", "a host of", "in the age of",
144: "gain a deeper understanding", "key drivers", "driving force",
145: "play a key role", "close the gap", "end-to-end", "to ensure that",
146: "it is essential to", "under the hood", "on the other hand" — missed the
147: ≥4× hot-vs-cold document-frequency lift or zero-hot floor against 8,290
148: GPT-4o continuations and 8,290 paired human controls. Do not re-add without
149: a newer paired-corpus lift report.
150: 
151: The phrases above are templated openers and closers AI uses to package
152: ordinary content as significant: "in today's [adjective] world", "the
153: ever-evolving landscape of X", "unlock the potential of Y", "play a crucial
154: role in Z". A single hit is forgivable; density is the signal — that is
155: what the density-per-1000-tokens threshold measures.
156: 
157: Adding entries: prefer phrases AI uses to scaffold significance over
158: content. Verify against HC3 ChatGPT samples before shipping. If a phrase
159: also fires in HC3 human at similar rate, drop it.
````

## File: patterns/en-communication.md
````markdown
 1: ---
 2: pack: en-communication
 3: language: en
 4: name: Communication Patterns
 5: version: 1.1.0
 6: patterns: 4
 7: corpus-snapshot:
 8:   id: bootstrap-patterns-pre-provenance
 9:   status: needs-quarterly-refresh
10:   source: maintainer-curated pattern packs before quarterly corpus snapshot tracking
11:   last_validated: null
12: ---
13: 
14: # Communication Patterns
15: 
16: ### 19. Collaborative Communication Artifacts
17: 
18: **Watch words:** I hope this helps!, Let me know if you need, Feel free to ask, Happy to help, Don't hesitate to reach out, I'd be glad to, Is there anything else, Hope that clarifies things, Let me know if you'd like me to
19: 
20: **Fire condition:** Any chatbot-style conversational phrase appears in content that is not a live interactive conversation.
21: 
22: **Exclusion:** Acceptable in transcripts of actual live chat sessions, intentional UI microcopy for chatbot interfaces, or dialogue being quoted and analyzed.
23: 
24: **Problem:** AI includes chatbot conversational phrases in written content. These are appropriate in a live chat but not in articles, reports, or documentation.
25: 
26: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
27: 
28: **Before:**
29: > The French Revolution began in 1789, driven by fiscal crisis and food shortages. I hope this helps! Let me know if you need more details on any specific aspect of the revolution. Feel free to ask about the key figures involved.
30: 
31: **After:**
32: > The French Revolution began in 1789, driven by fiscal crisis and food shortages. The immediate trigger was the near-bankruptcy of the French state and a bread price spike that hit Paris hardest.
33: 
34: ---
35: 
36: ### 20. Knowledge-Cutoff Disclaimers
37: 
38: **Watch words:** as of my last update, I don't have access to real-time, my training data, I cannot verify current, as of my knowledge cutoff, I'm not able to browse, please verify this information, this may have changed since
39: 
40: **Fire condition:** Any AI self-reference or training-data caveat appears in editorial, journalistic, or analytical content.
41: 
42: **Exclusion:** Acceptable in technical documentation explicitly about AI systems, or in content that intentionally discloses AI generation (with a proper disclosure note). Also acceptable if the caveat is replaced with a dated, source-cited fact.
43: 
44: **Problem:** AI includes training-data caveats in content that should not reference AI limitations. These disclaimers break the fourth wall and remind readers the text was machine-generated.
45: 
46: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
47: **Preservation Note:** The disclaimer sometimes carries a genuine factual caveat about data currency; removing it without replacing the claim with a dated source may leave an unverified assertion that the reader cannot assess.
48: 
49: **Before:**
50: > As of my last update in April 2024, the company had around 5,000 employees. I don't have access to real-time data, so please verify this information with current sources.
51: 
52: **After:**
53: > The company had about 5,000 employees as of its 2024 annual report.
54: 
55: ---
56: 
57: ### 21. Sycophantic/Servile Tone
58: 
59: **Watch words:** Great question!, That's an excellent point, You've raised a fascinating, What a thoughtful, That's a really interesting, Absolutely!, You're absolutely right, What a great observation
60: 
61: **Fire condition:** Any flattering or servile opener appears before substantive content.
62: 
63: **Exclusion:** None — this pattern has no valid context in editorial, analytical, or journalistic writing. Even in conversational formats, drop the flattery and start with the answer.
64: 
65: **Problem:** AI flatters the reader or questioner before answering. This servile opener adds no information and signals AI generation.
66: 
67: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
68: 
69: **Before:**
70: > Great question! That's a really fascinating topic. You've raised an excellent point about the economic factors at play. Let me break this down for you.
71: 
72: **After:**
73: > The main economic factor is the gap between housing supply and demand. Building permits in the metro area dropped 18% last year while population grew 2.1%.
74: 
75: ---
76: 
77: ### 29. False Nuance (Retroactive Reframing)
78: 
79: **Watch words:** Actually, it's more nuanced than that, To be more precise, Well, it's not quite that simple, That said, the reality is more complex, More accurately, In fairness, it's more that, If we're being precise, Though to be fair
80: 
81: **Fire condition:** The text restates or lightly reframes the preceding claim under the guise of adding nuance, without introducing new information, evidence, or a genuinely different perspective.
82: 
83: **Exclusion:** Acceptable when the reframe introduces a substantive correction, cites new evidence, or genuinely shifts the analytical frame in a way that changes the conclusion. Also acceptable in dialogue transcripts where the speaker is visibly self-correcting with new data.
84: 
85: **Problem:** AI hedges its own statements by immediately re-qualifying them in a way that sounds thoughtful but adds nothing. The "nuance" is cosmetic — the second sentence says the same thing as the first in slightly different words. This creates an illusion of depth while padding the text.
86: 
87: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
88: **Preservation Note:** Removing the reframing clause may delete a genuine qualification or exception; verify that the retained primary claim accurately stands alone before removing the "nuance" sentence.
89: 
90: **Before:**
91: > Remote work increases productivity. Actually, it's more nuanced than that — remote work can enhance productivity in certain contexts while presenting challenges in others, and the net effect depends on organizational culture and individual work styles.
92: 
93: **After:**
94: > Remote work increases productivity for focused solo tasks — a Stanford study found a 13% gain for call center workers. It hurts spontaneous collaboration, though: Microsoft's 2021 internal data showed cross-team communication dropped 25% after going fully remote.
````

## File: patterns/en-content.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: pack: en-content
  3: language: en
  4: name: Content Patterns
  5: version: 1.0.0
  6: patterns: 6
  7: corpus-snapshot:
  8:   id: bootstrap-patterns-pre-provenance
  9:   status: needs-quarterly-refresh
 10:   source: maintainer-curated pattern packs before quarterly corpus snapshot tracking
 11:   last_validated: null
 12: ---
 13: 
 14: # Content Patterns
 15: 
 16: ### 1. Undue Emphasis on Significance
 17: 
 18: **Watch words:** significant milestone, pivotal moment, groundbreaking, transformative, paradigm shift, revolutionary, game-changing, unprecedented, landmark achievement, watershed moment, trailblazing, monumental
 19: 
 20: **Fire condition:** 2+ emphasis words appear in the same paragraph, or a single word like "revolutionary" or "groundbreaking" applied to an ordinary product or event.
 21: 
 22: **Exclusion:** Genuine historical events of large scale (first moon landing, eradication of a disease) where the adjective is proportionate. Use judgment on actual impact.
 23: 
 24: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
 25: **Preservation Note:** Removing emphasis words may delete the author's actual claim about significance; over-correction can convert a genuinely notable event into an unremarkable one.
 26: 
 27: **Problem:** AI inflates the importance of ordinary topics. Everything becomes a "significant milestone" or a "paradigm shift," regardless of actual impact.
 28: 
 29: **Before:**
 30: > The company's new mobile app represents a groundbreaking paradigm shift in how users interact with grocery delivery services. This transformative, game-changing platform marks an unprecedented milestone in the retail industry.
 31: 
 32: **After:**
 33: > The company launched a grocery delivery app. It lets users schedule same-day deliveries and track orders in real time. Downloads hit 2 million in the first month.
 34: 
 35: ---
 36: 
 37: ### 2. Undue Emphasis on Notability/Media
 38: 
 39: **Watch words:** garnered significant attention, widely recognized, has been featured in, attracted widespread interest, gained international acclaim, made headlines, captured the imagination of, has been praised by critics and audiences alike
 40: 
 41: **Fire condition:** A claim of broad attention, coverage, or acclaim appears without a named publication, outlet, or specific figure.
 42: 
 43: **Exclusion:** Statements like "widely used" backed by a rough scale ("used in 50 countries", "over 10 million installs") — specificity makes them acceptable even without named sources.
 44: 
 45: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
 46: **Preservation Note:** Correcting unsourced attention claims may remove a core assertion about recognition or impact that the author intended as a factual claim.
 47: 
 48: **Problem:** AI claims broad media coverage or public attention without citing specific sources or evidence.
 49: 
 50: **Before:**
 51: > Her artwork has garnered significant attention from critics and audiences alike, and has been widely recognized as a defining voice of her generation. Her exhibitions have attracted widespread interest across the globe.
 52: 
 53: **After:**
 54: > The New York Times reviewed her 2023 exhibition, calling her use of recycled materials "quietly radical." The show sold out its three-week run at the Whitechapel Gallery.
 55: 
 56: ---
 57: 
 58: ### 3. Superficial -ing Analyses
 59: 
 60: **Watch words:** showcasing, highlighting, underscoring, demonstrating, illustrating, reflecting, signaling, exemplifying, reinforcing, embodying, encapsulating
 61: 
 62: **Fire condition:** 3+ present-participle phrases chained in a single sentence or consecutive clauses with no concrete causal explanation.
 63: 
 64: **Exclusion:** A single well-placed participle for genuine causal or temporal connection ("the policy increased costs, pushing firms to cut staff") is acceptable and not this pattern.
 65: 
 66: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
 67: **Preservation Note:** Replacing participle chains with concrete explanation restructures the argument; if the causal relationship is not well understood, the correction may introduce a claim the original did not make.
 68: 
 69: **Problem:** AI uses present participle chains as filler analysis. Instead of explaining *why* something matters, it strings together "-ing" words that gesture at significance without saying anything concrete.
 70: 
 71: **Before:**
 72: > The festival brings together artists from 30 countries, showcasing the diversity of contemporary dance, highlighting the importance of cross-cultural dialogue, and underscoring the role of the arts in fostering global understanding.
 73: 
 74: **After:**
 75: > The festival brings together artists from 30 countries. This year, a butoh troupe from Tokyo collaborated with a hip-hop crew from Lagos — a pairing that would not have happened without the festival's residency program.
 76: 
 77: ---
 78: 
 79: ### 4. Promotional Language
 80: 
 81: **Watch words:** stunning, breathtaking, world-class, gem of, hidden treasure, crown jewel, vibrant, nestled in, boasts, a must-visit, unparalleled, exquisite, awe-inspiring, picturesque
 82: 
 83: **Fire condition:** 2+ promotional adjectives modifying the same subject, or a single strong superlative ("world-class", "breathtaking", "must-visit") used as descriptive prose rather than quoted marketing copy.
 84: 
 85: **Exclusion:** Direct quotations from marketing or promotional materials being analyzed — the promotional language belongs to the source, not the author.
 86: 
 87: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 88: **Preservation Note:** Replacing promotional adjectives with neutral description may remove genuine qualitative assessments the author intended as their own voice or evaluation.
 89: 
 90: **Problem:** AI uses tourism-brochure language instead of neutral description, especially when writing about places, food, or cultural events.
 91: 
 92: **Before:**
 93: > Nestled in the rolling hills of Tuscany, this stunning village boasts breathtaking views, world-class cuisine, and an exquisite charm that makes it a must-visit hidden gem for any discerning traveler.
 94: 
 95: **After:**
 96: > The village sits on a hill about 40 minutes south of Florence. It has one restaurant, a weekly market on Thursdays, and a 14th-century church with frescoes that are slowly being restored.
 97: 
 98: ---
 99: 
100: ### 5. Vague Attributions
101: 
102: **Watch words:** experts say, many believe, it is widely accepted, studies show, research indicates, critics argue, according to sources, observers note, analysts predict, some suggest, it is generally agreed
103: 
104: **Fire condition:** Any claim of authority appears with an unspecified source rather than a named one (person, institution, publication, or study with date).
105: 
106: **Exclusion:** Well-established consensus facts with no reasonable controversy ("doctors say smoking causes lung cancer") — use judgment on whether a named source is genuinely necessary.
107: 
108: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
109: **Preservation Note:** Removing vague attributions without replacement deletes the author's sourcing claim; correction must substitute a real source or remove the claim entirely, which changes the text's evidentiary basis.
110: 
111: **Problem:** AI cites unnamed "experts" and "studies" instead of specific sources. This creates an illusion of authority without any verifiable backing.
112: 
113: **Before:**
114: > Experts say that remote work is here to stay. Studies show that productivity increases when employees work from home, and many believe this trend will reshape the commercial real estate market.
115: 
116: **After:**
117: > A 2023 Stanford study by Nicholas Bloom found that hybrid workers were 3% more productive than full-time office workers. Kastle Systems data shows U.S. office occupancy has stabilized at about 50% of pre-pandemic levels.
118: 
119: ---
120: 
121: ### 6. Formulaic "Challenges and Prospects"
122: 
123: **Watch words:** despite these challenges, remains to be seen, poised for growth, at a crossroads, on the cusp of, only time will tell, the road ahead, faces significant hurdles but, with continued effort, looking forward
124: 
125: **Fire condition:** A paragraph or conclusion contains both a generic challenge phrase AND a generic optimism phrase — the classic two-step pattern of acknowledging problems then pivoting to vague hope.
126: 
127: **Exclusion:** Genuine uncertainty expressed with specific caveats ("FDA approval takes 14 months; if denied by Q3, we shift to EU trials") — precision makes it acceptable. Only trigger when both poles are vague.
128: 
129: **Semantic Risk:** HIGH
130: **Preservation Note:** Replacing the challenge-prospect formula restructures the argument's conclusion; the corrected version must not omit actual challenges or prospects the author named, even vaguely.
131: 
132: **Problem:** AI wraps up with a generic challenges-then-optimism formula: acknowledge problems, then pivot to vague hope. This pattern appears at the end of almost every AI-generated article or essay.
133: 
134: **Before:**
135: > Despite these challenges, the industry remains poised for significant growth. While it remains to be seen how regulations will evolve, the sector stands at a crossroads, and with continued innovation and collaboration, a bright future lies ahead.
136: 
137: **After:**
138: > The biggest obstacle is the FDA approval timeline — the average wait is 14 months. Two of the five pending applications were filed before 2022 and still have no decision date. The company says it will shift trials to the EU if U.S. approval is not granted by Q3.
````

## File: patterns/en-filler.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: pack: en-filler
  3: language: en
  4: name: Filler & Hedging Patterns
  5: version: 1.1.0
  6: patterns: 4
  7: corpus-snapshot:
  8:   id: bootstrap-patterns-pre-provenance
  9:   status: needs-quarterly-refresh
 10:   source: maintainer-curated pattern packs before quarterly corpus snapshot tracking
 11:   last_validated: null
 12: ---
 13: 
 14: # Filler & Hedging Patterns
 15: 
 16: ### 22. Filler Phrases
 17: 
 18: **Watch words:** it's important to note that, it's worth mentioning that, it should be noted that, in order to, due to the fact that, at the end of the day, when it comes to, in terms of, the fact of the matter is, it goes without saying, needless to say, as a matter of fact, for all intents and purposes, in light of the fact that
 19: 
 20: **Fire condition:** 2+ filler phrases appear in the same paragraph, or a single phrase that can be deleted without any loss of meaning.
 21: 
 22: **Exclusion:** "In order to" is occasionally necessary for clarity in legal or formal regulatory writing where "to" could be misread as part of an infinitive chain. Use judgment in those contexts.
 23: 
 24: **Problem:** AI pads sentences with unnecessary filler that adds length but no meaning. These phrases can almost always be deleted or replaced with a shorter alternative.
 25: 
 26: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
 27: 
 28: **Substitutions:**
 29: - "it's important to note that" --> delete, just state the fact
 30: - "in order to" --> "to"
 31: - "due to the fact that" --> "because"
 32: - "at the end of the day" --> delete or rephrase
 33: - "when it comes to" --> delete or use the subject directly
 34: - "in terms of" --> delete or rephrase
 35: - "in light of the fact that" --> "because" or "since"
 36: 
 37: **Before:**
 38: > It's important to note that, in order to achieve sustainable growth, companies need to invest in R&D. Due to the fact that competition is increasing, it's worth mentioning that firms must also focus on talent retention. At the end of the day, when it comes to long-term success, innovation is key.
 39: 
 40: **After:**
 41: > Companies that want to grow need to invest in R&D. Competition for talent is rising just as fast — Gartner reports that tech attrition hit 20% last year.
 42: 
 43: ---
 44: 
 45: ### 23. Excessive Hedging
 46: 
 47: **Watch words:** it could potentially be argued that perhaps, may or may not, it is possible that, to some extent, in certain cases, it could be said that, there is a chance that, one might argue, it seems likely that, arguably, it is conceivable that, tends to suggest
 48: 
 49: **Fire condition:** 3+ qualifiers stacked on a single claim, or a statement so hedged it makes no falsifiable assertion.
 50: 
 51: **Exclusion:** A single hedge on a genuinely uncertain claim is appropriate and not this pattern ("this approach may reduce costs" when the evidence is preliminary). Only trigger when qualifiers are stacked or when the combined hedging evacuates all meaning.
 52: 
 53: **Problem:** AI over-qualifies every statement to avoid being wrong, producing text so hedged that it says almost nothing. A single hedge is fine; stacking three or four qualifiers on one claim is not.
 54: 
 55: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 56: **Preservation Note:** Removing hedges from a genuinely uncertain claim may produce an over-confident assertion; ensure the corrected version retains at least one qualifier if the underlying evidence is preliminary or contested.
 57: 
 58: **Before:**
 59: > It could potentially be argued that this approach may, to some extent, offer certain advantages in some cases, though it is possible that the results might vary depending on a number of factors that arguably remain to be fully understood.
 60: 
 61: **After:**
 62: > This approach has advantages, but results vary. The biggest variable is team size: teams under five people saw a 30% improvement, while larger teams saw little change.
 63: 
 64: ---
 65: 
 66: ### 24. Generic Positive Conclusions
 67: 
 68: **Watch words:** a bright future lies ahead, exciting journey, the possibilities are endless, stands as a testament, the sky is the limit, promising outlook, exciting times ahead, the best is yet to come, transformative potential, a new chapter
 69: 
 70: **Fire condition:** 2+ vague optimism phrases appear in the same paragraph or conclusion, or a standalone final sentence that is entirely optimism filler with no specific claim.
 71: 
 72: **Exclusion:** A specific optimistic statement backed by evidence ("given the trial data, FDA approval looks likely by Q3") is not this pattern — specificity makes it acceptable.
 73: 
 74: **Problem:** AI ends with vague optimism instead of specific conclusions. This pattern often appears alongside Pattern 6 (Formulaic Challenges and Prospects) but can also stand alone as a final sentence.
 75: 
 76: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
 77: 
 78: **Before:**
 79: > As the company enters this exciting new chapter, the possibilities are truly endless. With its talented team and innovative vision, a bright future undoubtedly lies ahead. The best is yet to come.
 80: 
 81: **After:**
 82: > The company plans to open two more locations by June and is hiring 15 engineers for its new climate-tech division.
 83: 
 84: **Closing formula before:**
 85: > In conclusion, this topic is one that will continue to generate discussion and debate for years to come. One thing is certain: the future of this field is full of promise and potential.
 86: 
 87: **Closing formula after:**
 88: > The bill goes to committee vote on March 12. If it passes, the new rules take effect in January.
 89: 
 90: **Not this pattern:** The "challenges, but bright future" two-step structure --> Pattern 6 (Formulaic "Challenges and Prospects," en-content.md) handles that.
 91: 
 92: ---
 93: 
 94: ### 31. Conclusion Signal Words
 95: 
 96: **Watch words:** In conclusion, Ultimately, In summary, To conclude, To sum up, Finally, All in all, In the end, Overall, On the whole
 97: 
 98: **Fire condition:** The document's final paragraph (or second-to-last) opens with one of the watch words. Or 2+ such conclusion signals appear across paragraphs.
 99: 
100: **Exclusion:**
101: - Academic papers with an explicit "Conclusion" section header (the header already signals; in-prose markers are redundant)
102: - Multi-step arguments where "Therefore" or "Thus" follows from a clear preceding chain (logic markers, not stylistic markers)
103: - Speech or lecture transcripts where the speaker explicitly signposts wrap-up
104: 
105: **Problem:** AI essays announce their conclusions ("In conclusion, X is Y") instead of just stating them. Human writers rarely flag the conclusion paragraph — they just write the conclusion. AI feels obligated to scaffold document structure for the reader.
106: 
107: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
108: **Preservation:** Removing only the signal word leaves a hollow paragraph. Rewrite the actual conclusion to be more concrete. The paragraph should still read as a conclusion without the marker.
109: 
110: **Burstiness guidance:** When removing conclusion signals, vary the replacement. Don't replace every "In conclusion, X is Y" with "X is Y, then" — that creates a new mechanical pattern. Mix short verdicts with longer explanatory wrap-ups. This pattern often co-fires with #24 (Generic Positive Conclusions); handle both together.
111: 
112: **Before:**
113: > In conclusion, work-life balance is not a luxury but a necessity. It is a critical foundation upon which sustainable success — both personal and professional — must be built.
114: 
115: **After:**
116: > Work-life balance isn't optional. Without it, the rest of the structure cracks.
117: 
118: **Not this pattern:** When the *content* of the conclusion is vague optimism --> Pattern 24 (Generic Positive Conclusions) handles that. Pattern 31 is about the signal *word itself* regardless of what follows.
````

## File: patterns/en-structure.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: pack: en-structure
  3: language: en
  4: name: Structure Patterns
  5: version: 1.2.0
  6: patterns: 5
  7: corpus-snapshot:
  8:   id: bootstrap-patterns-pre-provenance
  9:   status: needs-quarterly-refresh
 10:   source: maintainer-curated pattern packs before quarterly corpus snapshot tracking
 11:   last_validated: null
 12: phase: structure
 13: ---
 14: 
 15: # Structure Patterns
 16: 
 17: ---
 18: 
 19: ### 25. Metronomic Paragraph Structure
 20: 
 21: **Fire condition:** 3+ consecutive paragraphs follow the identical internal template — e.g., claim → evidence → significance, or problem → solution → benefit, or intro → development → conclusion.
 22: 
 23: **Exclusion:** Academic abstracts, legal briefs, and comparative reviews (product A vs. product B on the same criteria) where the repeated structure is the required format, not an AI artifact. Also exclude texts with 2 or fewer paragraphs.
 24: 
 25: **Burstiness note:** After breaking the structure, vary paragraph function deliberately. Some paragraphs should present pure evidence with no stated implication. Some should be a single-sentence observation. Some should open with a question, a contradiction, or a concrete detail rather than a topic claim.
 26: 
 27: **Problem:** AI stamps every paragraph with the same internal template regardless of what the content actually requires. Human writing varies paragraph shape: some paragraphs open with a question, some lead with a specific detail, some end without resolving anything. Metronomic regularity is one of the strongest structural signals of AI generation.
 28: 
 29: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 30: **Preservation Note:** Restructuring paragraph order or shape may alter the logical flow of an argument; verify that each paragraph's core claim survives the restructuring and that no evidence is dropped.
 31: 
 32: **Before:**
 33: > The electric vehicle market has experienced unprecedented growth in recent years. Sales doubled between 2021 and 2023, driven by falling battery costs and expanded charging infrastructure. This trend signals a fundamental shift in how consumers relate to personal transportation.
 34: >
 35: > Battery technology has improved considerably over the same period. Energy density has increased by 40% since 2018, while costs have fallen from $140 to $90 per kWh. These advances have made electric vehicles competitive with internal combustion alternatives on a total cost of ownership basis.
 36: >
 37: > Government policy has played a significant supporting role. Tax credits of up to $7,500 are available in the United States, and the EU has mandated a ban on new petrol vehicle sales by 2035. Such regulatory tailwinds are expected to sustain the sector's momentum into the next decade.
 38: 
 39: **After:**
 40: > Electric vehicle sales doubled between 2021 and 2023. The main driver isn't policy — it's battery prices, which fell from $140 to $90 per kWh.
 41: >
 42: > The US $7,500 tax credit helps, but it phases out once a manufacturer hits 200,000 vehicles sold. Tesla already passed that cap years ago.
 43: >
 44: > Whether the EU's 2035 petrol ban survives is genuinely unclear. Germany's coalition nearly collapsed over it in 2023, and three major automakers are lobbying for a 2040 date instead.
 45: 
 46: ---
 47: 
 48: ### 26. Passive Nominalization Chains
 49: 
 50: **Watch words:** was conducted, was performed, was developed, was established, were identified, is required, has been shown, is provided, are considered, is achieved, is determined, is utilized, is noted
 51: 
 52: **Fire condition:** 2+ passive nominalization phrases in the same paragraph — e.g., "an analysis was conducted", "consideration was given to", "a decision was made".
 53: 
 54: **Exclusion:** Scientific methods sections and formal regulatory documents where passive voice is a disciplinary norm. Also acceptable when the actor is genuinely unknown, irrelevant, or appropriately omitted.
 55: 
 56: **Burstiness note:** Replace passives with active verbs — but vary the subjects. Avoid making every sentence "We did X." Mix "The team found Y" with "Data showed Z" with bare declaratives that start with the finding itself.
 57: 
 58: **Problem:** AI chains passive nominalizations that convert active situations into abstract bureaucratic prose. "An analysis was conducted" means "we analyzed." The passive form removes agency and adds syllables without adding meaning.
 59: 
 60: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 61: **Preservation Note:** Converting passive to active assigns a specific agent; if the original intentionally omitted the actor (unknown, irrelevant, or sensitive), the active rewrite may introduce an unintended attribution.
 62: 
 63: **Before:**
 64: > An extensive review of the literature was conducted in order to identify key patterns. Consideration was given to a range of methodological approaches, and a decision was made to adopt a mixed-methods design. Data collection was performed over a six-month period, after which an analysis was undertaken to identify recurring themes.
 65: 
 66: **After:**
 67: > We reviewed 47 papers published between 2018 and 2024. Most used surveys; we chose interviews instead, since the population was small enough to go deep. Six months of interviews, then we coded the transcripts.
 68: 
 69: ---
 70: 
 71: ### 27. Zombie Nouns (Excessive Nominalization)
 72: 
 73: **Watch words:** make an improvement to, provide a description of, conduct an analysis of, give consideration to, offer an explanation of, achieve a reduction in, have an impact on, make a decision about, reach a conclusion regarding, perform an evaluation of, carry out an investigation into, give an indication of
 74: 
 75: **Fire condition:** 3+ nominalized verb phrases in the same paragraph — e.g., "make improvements" instead of "improve", "conduct an analysis" instead of "analyze".
 76: 
 77: **Exclusion:** Noun forms that carry meaning unavailable in the verb ("the investigation" as a noun refers to an ongoing process; "investigate" does not). Also acceptable in legal contexts where nominalized forms have specific technical definitions.
 78: 
 79: **Burstiness note:** Replace nominalized phrases with verbs, but not every instance. Keep one or two where the noun form fits naturally — avoid creating staccato prose by over-correcting.
 80: 
 81: **Problem:** AI converts verbs into bloated noun phrases, a pattern called "zombie nouns." Every action becomes an abstract event: "improve" becomes "make an improvement to," "analyze" becomes "conduct an analysis of." The result is wordy, impersonal, and slow.
 82: 
 83: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
 84: **Preservation Note:** Some nominalized forms carry distinct meaning from their verb counterparts ("the investigation" vs. "investigate"); verify that converting to a verb does not collapse a reference to an ongoing process or formal procedure.
 85: 
 86: **Before:**
 87: > The committee made a decision to conduct an analysis of the current situation and to provide a recommendation regarding future strategy. An evaluation of the available options was performed, and after careful consideration was given to each alternative, an agreement was reached on the most viable approach.
 88: 
 89: **After:**
 90: > The committee analyzed the situation and agreed on a strategy. They evaluated three options and chose the one with the lowest implementation risk.
 91: 
 92: ---
 93: 
 94: ### 28. Stacked Subordinate Clauses
 95: 
 96: **Fire condition:** A single sentence contains 3+ embedded relative clauses, appositives, or participial phrases before the main verb reaches its object — or the reader must parse 4+ commas before understanding the sentence's main claim.
 97: 
 98: **Exclusion:** Legal definitions and technical specifications occasionally require nested qualification. Academic writing may also stack clauses when the embedded information is non-negotiable to the sentence's meaning and cannot be separated without losing precision.
 99: 
100: **Burstiness note:** Break stacked sentences into two or three shorter ones. Start one sentence with the main clause, then add a separate sentence for the embedded detail. Vary lengths — don't make every resulting sentence the same.
101: 
102: **Problem:** AI builds sentences with cascading embedded clauses that delay the main verb and obscure the subject-verb relationship. The result is technically grammatical but exhausting to read and often contains no insight beyond what a shorter sentence would convey.
103: 
104: **Semantic Risk:** MEDIUM
105: **Preservation Note:** Breaking stacked clauses into separate sentences may lose precision embedded in the nested qualifications; ensure every embedded condition or qualification appears in the rewritten version.
106: 
107: **Before:**
108: > The initiative, which was developed in response to increasing concerns about, and growing awareness of, the urgent need to address the challenges facing both urban and rural communities in the context of rapid technological transformation, aims to bridge the gap between innovation and equitable access.
109: 
110: **After:**
111: > Rural and urban communities are getting left behind as technology changes fast. The initiative targets that gap directly — access to tools, not just the tools themselves.
112: 
113: ---
114: 
115: ### 30. Rhetorical Question Openers
116: 
117: **Problem:** AI essays open paragraphs with rhetorical questions ("So what does this mean?", "Why is this important?", "But how did we get here?") to scaffold structure. The writer already knows the answer; the question is filler that buys time before the actual claim. Real writers state observations directly. Rhetorical questions belong in speeches and editorials, not in informational essays.
118: 
119: **Trigger conditions:** A paragraph opens with an interrogative sentence ("Why...?", "How...?", "What...?", "But what if...?") AND the answer follows in the same or next sentence within the same paragraph. Or: the document contains 2+ rhetorical questions used as paragraph openers across paragraphs.
120: 
121: **Exceptions:**
122: - Interview, FAQ, or Q&A format documents
123: - Genuine open questions where the author truly doesn't know the answer (research questions, philosophical inquiry)
124: - Speech or lecture transcripts where audience engagement is the goal
125: - Academic papers' research question sections
126: 
127: **Semantic risk:** LOW
128: **Preservation:** Distinguish structural filler from genuinely emphatic questioning. If converting to declarative, verify the new sentence still carries the original emphasis.
129: 
130: **Burstiness guidance:** When converting rhetorical questions to declaratives, vary the result. Don't replace every "Why X?" with "X is because Y" — that just substitutes one mechanical pattern for another. Mix short emphatic statements with longer explanatory clauses.
131: 
132: **Before:**
133: > So why did Korean coffee culture grow so fast? The answer is surprisingly simple. Cafés moved beyond being places to buy drinks and became social hubs.
134: >
135: > But what about the future? Experts believe the trend will continue.
136: 
137: **After:**
138: > Korean coffee culture grew fast for a surprisingly simple reason: cafés moved beyond being places to buy drinks and became social hubs.
139: >
140: > The trend is likely to continue. Experts agree.
141: 
142: ---
143: 
144: ## English Burstiness Guidelines
145: 
146: Even without dedicated structure patterns, apply these burstiness principles when rewriting English text in Phase 1 (structure pass) or Phase 2 (sentence/lexical pass):
147: 
148: #### What is burstiness?
149: 
150: Human writing varies sentence length and structure unpredictably. AI writing is uniform: similar sentence length, consistent clause structure, even paragraph size. Burstiness means introducing deliberate irregularity.
151: 
152: #### How to apply
153: 
154: **Sentence length variation:**
155: - Mix short punchy sentences (4–8 words) with longer ones (20–35 words).
156: - A paragraph that opens with a one-sentence fragment and then unpacks in the next sentence reads as human.
157: - Avoid stretches of 4+ consecutive sentences with similar length.
158: 
159: **Paragraph length variation:**
160: - Paragraphs do not need to be 3–5 sentences. A single-sentence paragraph is fine for emphasis.
161: - Vary: short/long/medium, not medium/medium/medium.
162: 
163: **List count variation:**
164: - Do not default to 3-item lists. Use 2 items if there are 2, or 4 if there are 4.
165: - See Pattern #10 (Rule of Three) for correction details.
166: 
167: **Structure variation:**
168: - Mix declarative sentences, rhetorical questions, and sentence fragments.
169: - Not every sentence needs a subject-verb-object structure.
170: 
171: #### When to apply
172: 
173: Apply burstiness corrections when:
174: - All paragraphs are approximately the same length.
175: - Most sentences follow the same syntactic pattern (Subject + Verb + Object + subordinate clause).
176: - The overall rhythm feels metronomic (consistently even pacing).
177: 
178: Do **not** manufacture burstiness if the source text is already varied. Check before correcting.
````

## File: patterns/en-style.md
````markdown
  1: ---
  2: pack: en-style
  3: language: en
  4: name: Style Patterns
  5: version: 1.0.0
  6: patterns: 6
  7: corpus-snapshot:
  8:   id: bootstrap-patterns-pre-provenance
  9:   status: needs-quarterly-refresh
 10:   source: maintainer-curated pattern packs before quarterly corpus snapshot tracking
 11:   last_validated: null
 12: ---
 13: 
 14: # Style Patterns
 15: 
 16: ### 13. Em Dash Overuse
 17: 
 18: **Fire condition:** 1+ em dash appears in each of 2+ consecutive paragraphs, or the document-level em-dash count is high relative to sentence count.
 19: 
 20: **Exclusion:** Do not trigger on interrupted speech in quoted dialogue, glossary or dictionary-style separators, or deliberate literary style where one or two parenthetical em dashes are clearly intentional.
 21: 
 22: **Burstiness note:** When rewriting em-dash-heavy passages, distribute the repairs across commas, sentence breaks, parentheses, and colons. Uniform substitution (replacing every em dash with a comma) produces a new rhythm problem.
 23: 
 24: **Problem:** AI overuses em dashes in place of commas, parentheses, or subordinate clauses to create a polished effect. Human writers use them sparingly across a document; one em dash per paragraph over several paragraphs is a tell.
 25: 
 26: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
 27: 
 28: **Before:**
 29: > The pilot launched in March — a date chosen to align with the grant cycle.
 30: >
 31: > The first cohort included twelve clinics — each one serving a different rural county.
 32: >
 33: > Early feedback was positive — though the team still needs retention data before expanding.
 34: 
 35: **After:**
 36: > The pilot launched in March, a date chosen to align with the grant cycle.
 37: >
 38: > The first cohort included twelve clinics, each serving a different rural county.
 39: >
 40: > Early feedback was positive. The team still needs retention data before expanding.
 41: 
 42: ---
 43: 
 44: ### 14. Boldface Overuse
 45: 
 46: **Fire condition:** 5+ bolded terms in a single document, or 3+ in a single paragraph.
 47: 
 48: **Exclusion:** Bold is appropriate in UI documentation (button names, field labels), safety warnings ("**Do not** delete this file"), and genuine reference material where bold signals terminology. Only trigger in flowing prose.
 49: 
 50: **Problem:** AI bolds key terms as if writing a study guide or corporate slide deck. In normal prose, bolding should be rare or absent.
 51: 
 52: **Semantic Risk:** LOW
 53: 
 54: **Before:**
 55: > **Machine learning** is a subset of **artificial intelligence** that uses **statistical methods** to enable computers to **learn from data**. The most common approaches include **supervised learning**, **unsupervised learning**, and **reinforcement learning**.
 56: 
 57: **After:**
 58: > Machine learning is a subset of artificial intelligence that uses statistical methods to let computers learn from data. The three main approaches are supervised learning, unsupervised learning, and reinforcement learning.
 59: 
 60: ---
 61: 
 62: ### 15. Inline-Header Vertical Lists
 63:
