## File: src/features/lexicon-core.js
````javascript
  1: // Browser-pure AI-lexicon parsing and density helpers per core/stylometry.md §16.
  2: 
  3: export const DEFAULT_LEXICON_DENSITY_THRESHOLD = 3.0;
  4: export const DEFAULT_LEXICON_MIN_HOT_MATCHES = {
  5:   default: 1,
  6:   ko: 2,
  7:   zh: 2,
  8:   ja: 2,
  9: };
 10: 
 11: // Parses the two well-known sections out of a lexicon markdown file.
 12: // Returns { strict: string[], phrases: string[] }.
 13: export function parseLexiconBody(body, { skipUnderscore = false } = {}) {
 14:   const strict = [];
 15:   const phrases = [];
 16:   let mode = null;
 17:   for (const rawLine of body.split('\n')) {
 18:     const line = rawLine.trim();
 19:     if (line.startsWith('## ')) {
 20:       const heading = line.toLowerCase();
 21:       if (heading.includes('strict matches')) mode = 'strict';
 22:       else if (heading.includes('multi-word phrases')) mode = 'phrases';
 23:       else mode = null;
 24:       continue;
 25:     }
 26:     if (mode && line.startsWith('- ')) {
 27:       // Normalize to NFC so visually identical entries don't fail to match
 28:       // tokens that arrive in a different normalization form.
 29:       const entry = line.slice(2).trim().normalize('NFC');
 30:       if (entry && (!skipUnderscore || !entry.startsWith('_'))) {
 31:         (mode === 'strict' ? strict : phrases).push(entry);
 32:       }
 33:     }
 34:   }
 35:   return { strict, phrases };
 36: }
 37: 
 38: // Phrases may include `~` as a wildcard standing in for up to 40 chars.
 39: // Inter-word whitespace becomes `\s+` and the wildcard becomes `[\s\S]{0,40}`
 40: // so a phrase hard-wrapped across a single newline inside a paragraph (which
 41: // only blank lines split) still matches instead of silently undercounting.
 42: export function phraseToRegex(phrase) {
 43:   // Collapse runs of consecutive wildcards (optionally whitespace-separated)
 44:   // into a single `~`. Adjacent `[\s\S]{0,40}` / `\s+` quantifiers overlap and
 45:   // backtrack exponentially on long inputs — a ReDoS reachable only via a
 46:   // hand-authored custom lexicon, but cheap to neutralize here (#527 H12).
 47:   const collapsed = String(phrase).replace(/~(?:\s*~)+/g, '~');
 48:   const escaped = collapsed
 49:     .toLowerCase()
 50:     .replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')
 51:     .replace(/\s+/g, '\\s+');
 52:   const withWildcard = escaped.replace(/~/g, '[\\s\\S]{0,40}');
 53:   return new RegExp(withWildcard);
 54: }
 55: 
 56: // Whole-word match for a multi-token non-CJK strict entry (contains a space,
 57: // hyphen, or apostrophe). Anchored on Unicode letter/digit boundaries so it
 58: // cannot hit inside a larger token, and whitespace tolerates soft line wraps.
 59: function strictEntryRegex(lowerEntry) {
 60:   const escaped = lowerEntry
 61:     .replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&')
 62:     .replace(/\s+/g, '\\s+');
 63:   return new RegExp(`(?<![\\p{L}\\p{N}])${escaped}(?![\\p{L}\\p{N}])`, 'u');
 64: }
 65: 
 66: // Per-lexicon regex caches keyed by the lexicon object identity. Phrase and
 67: // strict-entry regexes depend only on the lexicon's entry text, never on the
 68: // paragraph under test, so they are compiled once per (lexicon, entry) pair
 69: // and reused across every computeDensity() call. WeakMaps keep the cache OFF
 70: // the lexicon object — Object.keys(lexicon) and JSON.stringify(lexicon) are
 71: // unchanged, and a lexicon's cache is garbage-collected with it. The compiled
 72: // regexes are non-global and non-sticky (no /g or /y flags), so repeated
 73: // .test() calls carry no lastIndex state and are safe to share. Entries are
 74: // keyed by string, so any later-added phrase/entry is compiled lazily.
 75: const phraseRegexCache = new WeakMap();
 76: const strictEntryRegexCache = new WeakMap();
 77: 
 78: function memoizedRegex(cache, lexicon, key, build) {
 79:   let byKey = cache.get(lexicon);
 80:   if (!byKey) {
 81:     byKey = new Map();
 82:     cache.set(lexicon, byKey);
 83:   }
 84:   let regex = byKey.get(key);
 85:   if (!regex) {
 86:     regex = build(key);
 87:     byKey.set(key, regex);
 88:   }
 89:   return regex;
 90: }
 91: 
 92: // Counts paragraph-level lexicon hits. Strict entries match whole-word
 93: // (Unicode-aware boundaries via \p{L}\p{N}); phrases match as substrings
 94: // with `~` wildcard support. Each entry counts at most once per paragraph.
 95: export function computeDensity(paragraphText, tokens, lexicon) {
 96:   const lowerText = paragraphText.toLowerCase();
 97:   const hits = [];
 98:   const tokenSet = new Set(tokens.map((t) => t.toLowerCase()));
 99: 
100:   // §16: English strict entries match whole-word; CJK strict entries are
101:   // approximated by substring. Korean inflection and zh/ja character fallback
102:   // mean `자리매김`, `可以说`, or `まとめると` may not survive as whole tokens, so
103:   // CJK keeps bare substring matching. A non-CJK strict entry that is not a
104:   // single whole token (it carries a space/hyphen/apostrophe, so tokenization
105:   // split or edge-stripped it) falls back to a whole-word boundary-anchored
106:   // match — never bare substring, which would hit inside a larger token and
107:   // break the documented whole-word contract (lexicon/ai-en.md).
108:   const cjkSubstring = ['ko', 'zh', 'ja'].includes(lexicon.lang);
109:   for (const entry of lexicon.strict) {
110:     const lowerEntry = entry.toLowerCase();
111:     if (tokenSet.has(lowerEntry)) {
112:       hits.push(entry);
113:       continue;
114:     }
115:     // lexicon/ai-en.md documents "A trailing `s` or `ing` form counts as the
116:     // same entry" for English strict entries, but only the exact token was
117:     // ever matched (#566). Accept the two documented inflections for non-CJK
118:     // single-token entries; CJK strict matching is substring-based below and
119:     // Korean inflections are absorbed by phrase matching per the same doc.
120:     if (!cjkSubstring && (tokenSet.has(`${lowerEntry}s`) || tokenSet.has(`${lowerEntry}ing`))) {
121:       hits.push(entry);
122:       continue;
123:     }
124:     const isMultiToken = /[^\p{L}\p{N}]/u.test(lowerEntry);
125:     if (cjkSubstring) {
126:       if (lowerText.includes(lowerEntry)) hits.push(entry);
127:     } else if (isMultiToken && memoizedRegex(strictEntryRegexCache, lexicon, lowerEntry, strictEntryRegex).test(lowerText)) {
128:       hits.push(entry);
129:     }
130:   }
131:   for (const phrase of lexicon.phrases) {
132:     if (memoizedRegex(phraseRegexCache, lexicon, phrase, phraseToRegex).test(lowerText)) hits.push(phrase);
133:   }
134: 
135:   const density = tokens.length > 0 ? (hits.length / tokens.length) * 1000 : 0;
136:   return { matches: hits.length, density, hits };
137: }
138: 
139: /**
140:  * @param {{ matches?: number, density?: number }} [lexiconStats]
141:  * @param {{ lang?: string, densityThreshold?: number, minHotMatches?: (number|Record<string, number>) }} [options]
142:  */
143: export function classifyLexiconHot(
144:   lexiconStats,
145:   {
146:     lang,
147:     densityThreshold = DEFAULT_LEXICON_DENSITY_THRESHOLD,
148:     minHotMatches = DEFAULT_LEXICON_MIN_HOT_MATCHES,
149:   } = {}
150: ) {
151:   const matches = lexiconStats?.matches ?? 0;
152:   const density = lexiconStats?.density ?? 0;
153:   const minMatches = resolveMinHotMatches(lang, minHotMatches);
154:   return matches >= minMatches && density > densityThreshold;
155: }
156: 
157: export function resolveMinHotMatches(lang, minHotMatches) {
158:   if (typeof minHotMatches === 'number' && Number.isFinite(minHotMatches)) {
159:     return Math.max(1, minHotMatches);
160:   }
161:   const normalized = typeof lang === 'string' ? lang.toLowerCase() : 'default';
162:   const value = minHotMatches?.[normalized] ?? minHotMatches?.default;
163:   return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? Math.max(1, value) : 1;
164: }
````

## File: src/features/lexicon.js
````javascript
 1: // AI-lexicon loading per core/stylometry.md §16.
 2: // Loads the markdown lexicon at lexicon/ai-{lang}.md. Custom lexicons under
 3: // custom/lexicon/ take precedence when present.
 4: 
 5: import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
 6: import { resolve } from 'node:path';
 7: import { parseLexiconBody } from './lexicon-core.js';
 8: 
 9: export {
10:   DEFAULT_LEXICON_DENSITY_THRESHOLD,
11:   DEFAULT_LEXICON_MIN_HOT_MATCHES,
12:   parseLexiconBody,
13:   phraseToRegex,
14:   computeDensity,
15:   classifyLexiconHot,
16:   resolveMinHotMatches,
17: } from './lexicon-core.js';
18: 
19: export function loadLexicon(lang, repoRoot) {
20:   const candidates = [
21:     resolve(repoRoot, 'custom', 'lexicon', `ai-${lang}.md`),
22:     resolve(repoRoot, 'lexicon', `ai-${lang}.md`),
23:   ];
24:   for (const path of candidates) {
25:     if (existsSync(path)) {
26:       const raw = readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
27:       const body = raw.replace(/^---[\s\S]*?---\s*/, '');
28:       return { lang, path, ...parseLexiconBody(body) };
29:     }
30:   }
31:   return { lang, path: null, strict: [], phrases: [] };
32: }
````

## File: src/features/segment.js
````javascript
  1: // Paragraph / sentence / token splitting per core/stylometry.md §2 §3.
  2: //
  3: // Tokenization is intentionally simple: whitespace split + edge-punctuation
  4: // strip, no morphological analysis. For Chinese/Japanese prose, where normal
  5: // text often has no whitespace, use a deterministic character-token fallback
  6: // so sentence-length and lexical-diversity signals are not collapsed to
  7: // "one token per sentence." Sentence splitting is regex-only and accepts known
  8: // false splits on abbreviations / decimals (documented limit).
  9: //
 10: // A CJK sentence terminator (。！？) is treated as a boundary only when it is
 11: // NOT immediately followed by a closing quote/bracket, so quote-internal
 12: // terminators (彼は「やめろ。」と言った。) do not split mid-quote and a trailing
 13: // closer (…と言った。」) is never stranded as its own zero-token "sentence".
 14: // U+2026 (…) is intentionally NOT a hard terminator: in en/ko it is usually
 15: // intra-sentence ("Well… maybe not."), so splitting on it would perturb token
 16: // counts on exactly the human prose the tool must not flag. It is still
 17: // stripped from sentence ends below.
 18: const SENTENCE_CLOSERS = '」』】〕）》〉｝"\'”’';
 19: const SENTENCE_SPLIT_RE = new RegExp(
 20:   `[.!?]+\\s+|(?<=[。！？])(?![${SENTENCE_CLOSERS}])|\\n+`,
 21:   'u'
 22: );
 23: const PARAGRAPH_SPLIT_RE = /\n\s*\n/;
 24: const LIST_LINE_RE = /^\s*(?:[-*+]\s+|\d+[.)]\s+)/u;
 25: // \W in Unicode-aware mode. Strips edge punctuation but keeps internal
 26: // hyphens / apostrophes (e.g. "don't", "좋은-도구") as a single token.
 27: const EDGE_PUNCT_RE = /^[^\p{L}\p{N}]+|[^\p{L}\p{N}]+$/gu;
 28: const CJK_TOKEN_RE = /[\p{Script=Han}\p{Script=Hiragana}\p{Script=Katakana}\u30FC]|[A-Za-z0-9]+/gu;
 29: 
 30: export function splitParagraphs(text) {
 31:   if (!text) return [];
 32:   return text
 33:     .split(PARAGRAPH_SPLIT_RE)
 34:     .map((p) => p.trim())
 35:     .filter((p) => p.length > 0);
 36: }
 37: 
 38: function stripListBlocks(paragraph) {
 39:   const lines = String(paragraph ?? '').split(/\r?\n/);
 40:   const proseLines = [];
 41:   let colonListRemaining = 0;
 42:   for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) {
 43:     const rawLine = lines[i];
 44:     const trimmed = rawLine.trim();
 45:     if (trimmed === '') {
 46:       colonListRemaining = 0;
 47:       proseLines.push(rawLine);
 48:       continue;
 49:     }
 50:     if (LIST_LINE_RE.test(rawLine)) continue;
 51:     if (colonListRemaining > 0) {
 52:       colonListRemaining--;
 53:       continue;
 54:     }
 55:     if (trimmed.endsWith(':')) {
 56:       colonListRemaining = countFollowingPlainListLines(lines, i + 1);
 57:     }
 58:     proseLines.push(rawLine);
 59:   }
 60:   return proseLines.join('\n');
 61: }
 62: 
 63: function countFollowingPlainListLines(lines, start) {
 64:   let count = 0;
 65:   for (let i = start; i < lines.length; i++) {
 66:     const trimmed = lines[i].trim();
 67:     if (trimmed === '') break;
 68:     if (LIST_LINE_RE.test(lines[i])) continue;
 69:     count++;
 70:   }
 71:   return count >= 2 ? count : 0;
 72: }
 73: 
 74: export function splitSentences(paragraph) {
 75:   if (!paragraph) return [];
 76:   return paragraph
 77:     .split(SENTENCE_SPLIT_RE)
 78:     .map((s) => s.trim().replace(/[.!?。！？…]+$/u, ''))
 79:     .filter((s) => s.length > 0);
 80: }
 81: 
 82: export function splitProseSentences(paragraph) {
 83:   return splitSentences(stripListBlocks(paragraph));
 84: }
 85: 
 86: function tokenizeCjk(text) {
 87:   const tokens = [];
 88:   for (const match of text.matchAll(CJK_TOKEN_RE)) {
 89:     const token = match[0].replace(EDGE_PUNCT_RE, '');
 90:     if (token) tokens.push(token);
 91:   }
 92:   return tokens;
 93: }
 94: 
 95: export function tokenize(text, opts = {}) {
 96:   if (!text) return [];
 97:   if (opts.lang === 'zh' || opts.lang === 'ja') return tokenizeCjk(text);
 98:   return text
 99:     .split(/\s+/)
100:     .map((chunk) => chunk.replace(EDGE_PUNCT_RE, ''))
101:     .filter((t) => t.length > 0);
102: }
````

## File: src/features/structural-classifier.js
````javascript
  1: // Small, dependency-free logistic classifier helpers for optional structural
  2: // stylometry models. The public package ships the code path, not trained private
  3: // weights; callers must provide a model explicitly.
  4: import { extractStructuralFeatures, STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES } from './structural-features.js';
  5: 
  6: export const DEFAULT_STRUCTURAL_THRESHOLD = 0.5;
  7: 
  8: function sigmoid(value) {
  9:   return 1 / (1 + Math.exp(-value));
 10: }
 11: 
 12: function assertMatrix(X) {
 13:   if (!Array.isArray(X) || X.length === 0 || !Array.isArray(X[0]) || X[0].length === 0) {
 14:     throw new TypeError('training features must be a non-empty matrix');
 15:   }
 16:   const width = X[0].length;
 17:   for (const row of X) {
 18:     if (!Array.isArray(row) || row.length !== width || row.some((value) => !Number.isFinite(value))) {
 19:       throw new TypeError('training features must be rectangular finite-number rows');
 20:     }
 21:   }
 22: }
 23: 
 24: function assertLabels(y, expectedLength) {
 25:   if (!Array.isArray(y) || y.length !== expectedLength || y.some((value) => value !== 0 && value !== 1)) {
 26:     throw new TypeError('training labels must be 0/1 values aligned to features');
 27:   }
 28: }
 29: 
 30: export function fitScaler(X) {
 31:   assertMatrix(X);
 32:   const width = X[0].length;
 33:   const mu = new Array(width).fill(0);
 34:   const sigma = new Array(width).fill(0);
 35: 
 36:   for (const row of X) {
 37:     for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) mu[j] += row[j];
 38:   }
 39:   for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) mu[j] /= X.length;
 40: 
 41:   for (const row of X) {
 42:     for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) sigma[j] += (row[j] - mu[j]) ** 2;
 43:   }
 44:   for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) sigma[j] = Math.sqrt(sigma[j] / X.length) || 1;
 45: 
 46:   return { mu, sigma };
 47: }
 48: 
 49: export function applyScaler({ mu, sigma }, row) {
 50:   if (!Array.isArray(mu) || !Array.isArray(sigma) || !Array.isArray(row)) {
 51:     throw new TypeError('scaler and row must be arrays');
 52:   }
 53:   if (mu.length !== sigma.length || row.length !== mu.length) {
 54:     throw new TypeError('scaler dimensions must match row dimensions');
 55:   }
 56:   return row.map((value, index) => (value - mu[index]) / sigma[index]);
 57: }
 58: 
 59: export function trainLogReg(X, y, { lr = 0.1, epochs = 2000, l2 = 0.01 } = {}) {
 60:   assertMatrix(X);
 61:   assertLabels(y, X.length);
 62:   if (!Number.isFinite(lr) || lr <= 0) throw new TypeError('lr must be a positive finite number');
 63:   if (!Number.isInteger(epochs) || epochs <= 0) throw new TypeError('epochs must be a positive integer');
 64:   if (!Number.isFinite(l2) || l2 < 0) throw new TypeError('l2 must be a non-negative finite number');
 65: 
 66:   const scaler = fitScaler(X);
 67:   const Z = X.map((row) => applyScaler(scaler, row));
 68:   const width = Z[0].length;
 69:   const w = new Array(width).fill(0);
 70:   let b = 0;
 71: 
 72:   for (let epoch = 0; epoch < epochs; epoch++) {
 73:     const gradientW = new Array(width).fill(0);
 74:     let gradientB = 0;
 75: 
 76:     for (let i = 0; i < Z.length; i++) {
 77:       const probability = sigmoid(Z[i].reduce((sum, value, index) => sum + value * w[index], b));
 78:       const error = probability - y[i];
 79:       for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) gradientW[j] += error * Z[i][j];
 80:       gradientB += error;
 81:     }
 82: 
 83:     for (let j = 0; j < width; j++) w[j] -= lr * (gradientW[j] / Z.length + l2 * w[j]);
 84:     b -= lr * (gradientB / Z.length);
 85:   }
 86: 
 87:   // Only stamp this patina version's feature names onto models actually
 88:   // trained at that width; a toy/experimental model must not masquerade as a
 89:   // STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES-compatible model (normalizeStructuralModel
 90:   // rejects non-matching widths at load time, issue #436).
 91:   return width === STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.length
 92:     ? { weights: w, bias: b, scaler, featureNames: STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES }
 93:     : { weights: w, bias: b, scaler };
 94: }
 95: 
 96: export function normalizeStructuralModel(model) {
 97:   if (!model || typeof model !== 'object') return null;
 98:   const weights = model.weights ?? model.w;
 99:   const bias = model.bias ?? model.b;
100:   const scaler = model.scaler;
101:   const threshold = model.threshold ?? DEFAULT_STRUCTURAL_THRESHOLD;
102: 
103:   if (!Array.isArray(weights) || !Number.isFinite(bias) || !scaler || !Number.isFinite(threshold)) {
104:     throw new TypeError('structural model requires weights, bias, scaler, and optional threshold');
105:   }
106:   if (!Array.isArray(scaler.mu) || !Array.isArray(scaler.sigma)) {
107:     throw new TypeError('structural model scaler requires mu and sigma arrays');
108:   }
109:   if (weights.length !== scaler.mu.length || weights.length !== scaler.sigma.length) {
110:     throw new TypeError('structural model dimensions must match scaler dimensions');
111:   }
112:   // Dimension must match THIS patina version's feature extractor, even when
113:   // the optional featureNames field is absent (issue #436). Otherwise a
114:   // self-consistent model trained against an older feature set loads cleanly
115:   // and only explodes at predict time, deep inside analyzeText — past the
116:   // call sites that handle load errors (audit aborts untyped; scoring.js
117:   // silently zeroes the whole deterministic shadow score, including the
118:   // markup-leakage floor).
119:   if (weights.length !== STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.length) {
120:     throw new TypeError(
121:       `structural model expects ${weights.length} features but this patina version extracts ${STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.length} (${STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.join(', ')}); retrain the model against the current feature set`,
122:     );
123:   }
124:   if (weights.some((value) => !Number.isFinite(value)) || scaler.mu.some((value) => !Number.isFinite(value)) || scaler.sigma.some((value) => !Number.isFinite(value))) {
125:     throw new TypeError('structural model values must be finite numbers');
126:   }
127:   // Sigma must be strictly positive: a hand-written or truncated model with a
128:   // zero/negative sigma passes the finite check but makes applyScaler emit
129:   // NaN/±Infinity, which sigmoid turns into a silent NaN verdict (#443).
130:   if (scaler.sigma.some((value) => !(value > 0))) {
131:     throw new TypeError('structural model scaler sigma values must be positive');
132:   }
133:   if (Array.isArray(model.featureNames) && model.featureNames.join('\0') !== STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.join('\0')) {
134:     throw new TypeError('structural model featureNames do not match this patina version');
135:   }
136: 
137:   return {
138:     ...model,
139:     weights,
140:     bias,
141:     scaler,
142:     threshold,
143:     featureNames: STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES,
144:   };
145: }
146: 
147: export function predictStructuralScore(model, row) {
148:   const normalized = normalizeStructuralModel(model);
149:   if (!normalized) throw new TypeError('structural model is required');
150:   const scaled = applyScaler(normalized.scaler, row);
151:   const score = sigmoid(scaled.reduce((sum, value, index) => sum + value * normalized.weights[index], normalized.bias));
152:   return score;
153: }
154: 
155: export function thresholdForMaxFpr(model, Xtrain, ytrain, maxFpr = 0.1) {
156:   const normalized = normalizeStructuralModel(model);
157:   assertMatrix(Xtrain);
158:   assertLabels(ytrain, Xtrain.length);
159:   if (!Number.isFinite(maxFpr) || maxFpr < 0 || maxFpr > 1) {
160:     throw new TypeError('maxFpr must be a finite number between 0 and 1');
161:   }
162:   const negativeScores = Xtrain
163:     .filter((_, index) => ytrain[index] === 0)
164:     .map((row) => predictStructuralScore(normalized, row))
165:     .sort((a, b) => a - b);
166:   if (negativeScores.length === 0) return DEFAULT_STRUCTURAL_THRESHOLD;
167:   // ceil (not floor) so the realized FPR never exceeds maxFpr: floor would keep
168:   // an extra negative above the threshold (#443).
169:   const index = Math.min(negativeScores.length - 1, Math.ceil((1 - maxFpr) * negativeScores.length));
170:   return Math.max(DEFAULT_STRUCTURAL_THRESHOLD, negativeScores[index]);
171: }
172: 
173: export function structuralModelVerdict(text, { lang = 'ko', model = null } = {}) {
174:   if (!model) return { available: false, hot: null, score: null };
175:   const normalized = normalizeStructuralModel(model);
176:   // A truthy non-object model normalizes to null; treat as unavailable rather
177:   // than null-derefing normalized.lang (#443).
178:   if (!normalized || (normalized.lang && normalized.lang !== lang)) {
179:     return { available: false, hot: null, score: null };
180:   }
181:   const row = extractStructuralFeatures(text, { lang });
182:   const score = predictStructuralScore(normalized, row);
183:   return {
184:     available: true,
185:     hot: score >= normalized.threshold,
186:     score: Number(score.toFixed(4)),
187:   };
188: }
````

## File: src/features/structural-features.js
````javascript
  1: // Document-level structural features shared by the public analyzer and hosted
  2: // services. These are model-agnostic stylometry signals: no corpus rows, labels,
  3: // trained weights, prompts, or private assets live here.
  4: import { splitParagraphs, splitProseSentences, tokenize } from './segment.js';
  5: import { burstinessCV, mattr } from './stylometry.js';
  6: 
  7: export const STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES = Object.freeze([
  8:   'burstiness_cv',
  9:   'mean_sent_len',
 10:   'std_sent_len',
 11:   'ttr',
 12:   'mattr',
 13:   'hapax_ratio',
 14:   'mean_tok_len',
 15:   'comma_per_sent',
 16:   'punct_ratio',
 17:   'ending_diversity',
 18:   'connective_ratio',
 19:   'digit_ratio',
 20: ]);
 21: 
 22: const KO_CONNECTIVES = new Set([
 23:   '또한',
 24:   '게다가',
 25:   '뿐만',
 26:   '따라서',
 27:   '그러나',
 28:   '하지만',
 29:   '결론적으로',
 30:   '즉',
 31:   '특히',
 32:   '한편',
 33:   '그리고',
 34:   '그래서',
 35:   '때문에',
 36:   '통해',
 37:   '위해',
 38:   '대한',
 39:   '다양한',
 40:   '효과적으로',
 41:   '중요하다고',
 42:   '있습니다',
 43:   '바랍니다',
 44: ]);
 45: 
 46: function mean(values) {
 47:   return values.length ? values.reduce((sum, value) => sum + value, 0) / values.length : 0;
 48: }
 49: 
 50: function stddev(values) {
 51:   if (values.length < 2) return 0;
 52:   const avg = mean(values);
 53:   return Math.sqrt(mean(values.map((value) => (value - avg) ** 2)));
 54: }
 55: 
 56: function countMatches(values, predicate) {
 57:   let count = 0;
 58:   for (const value of values) {
 59:     if (predicate(value)) count += 1;
 60:   }
 61:   return count;
 62: }
 63: 
 64: /**
 65:  * Extract a numeric vector aligned with STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES.
 66:  *
 67:  * @param {string} text Text to analyze.
 68:  * @param {{lang?: string, mattrWindow?: number}} [opts] Language/tokenizer options.
 69:  * @returns {number[]} Numeric feature vector.
 70:  */
 71: export function extractStructuralFeatures(text, { lang = 'ko', mattrWindow = 40 } = {}) {
 72:   const normalized = (text || '').normalize('NFC');
 73:   const paragraphs = splitParagraphs(normalized);
 74:   const sentences = paragraphs.flatMap((paragraph) => splitProseSentences(paragraph));
 75:   const sentenceTokens = sentences.map((sentence) => tokenize(sentence, { lang }));
 76:   const sentenceLengths = sentenceTokens.map((tokens) => tokens.length).filter((count) => count > 0);
 77:   const tokens = sentenceTokens.flat();
 78:   const types = new Set(tokens);
 79: 
 80:   const frequencies = new Map();
 81:   for (const token of tokens) frequencies.set(token, (frequencies.get(token) || 0) + 1);
 82:   const hapaxCount = countMatches(frequencies.values(), (count) => count === 1);
 83: 
 84:   const compactChars = [...normalized.replace(/\s/g, '')];
 85:   const punctCount = countMatches(compactChars, (char) => /[.,!?;:·…"'()[\]{}—\-~]/.test(char));
 86:   const digitCount = countMatches(compactChars, (char) => /[0-9]/.test(char));
 87:   const commaCount = (normalized.match(/[,，]/g) || []).length;
 88: 
 89:   const endings = sentences
 90:     .map((sentence) => {
 91:       const trimmed = sentence.trim();
 92:       return trimmed.slice(-3).replace(/[.!?]/g, '').slice(-2);
 93:     })
 94:     .filter(Boolean);
 95:   const endingDiversity = sentences.length ? new Set(endings).size / sentences.length : 0;
 96:   const connectiveHits = countMatches(tokens, (token) => KO_CONNECTIVES.has(token));
 97: 
 98:   return [
 99:     sentenceLengths.length >= 2 ? burstinessCV(sentenceLengths) : 0,
100:     mean(sentenceLengths),
101:     stddev(sentenceLengths),
102:     tokens.length ? types.size / tokens.length : 0,
103:     mattr(tokens, mattrWindow) ?? 0,
104:     types.size ? hapaxCount / types.size : 0,
105:     tokens.length ? mean(tokens.map((token) => token.length)) : 0,
106:     sentences.length ? commaCount / sentences.length : 0,
107:     compactChars.length ? punctCount / compactChars.length : 0,
108:     endingDiversity,
109:     tokens.length ? connectiveHits / tokens.length : 0,
110:     compactChars.length ? digitCount / compactChars.length : 0,
111:   ];
112: }
113: 
114: /**
115:  * Pair a feature vector with names for logging/debug output.
116:  *
117:  * @param {number[]} vector Numeric feature vector.
118:  * @param {readonly string[]} [names] Feature names aligned to vector positions.
119:  * @returns {Record<string, number>}
120:  */
121: export function structuralFeatureRecord(vector, names = STRUCTURAL_FEATURE_NAMES) {
122:   /** @type {Record<string, number>} */
123:   const record = {};
124:   for (let i = 0; i < names.length; i++) record[names[i]] = vector[i] ?? 0;
125:   return record;
126: }
````

## File: src/features/stylometry.js
````javascript
  1: // Burstiness CV, MATTR, and dependency-free KO diagnostics per core/stylometry.md.
  2: // Pure functions over token arrays; no I/O.
  3: import { splitParagraphs, splitProseSentences } from './segment.js';
  4: // Interference regexes are owned by catalog/ko-interference.js — never inline
  5: // a copy here; the catalog-consumption test pins these call sites.
  6: import { buildKoInterferenceRegex } from './catalog/ko-interference.js';
  7: 
  8: export const DEFAULT_BURSTINESS_BANDS = { low: 0.30, high: 0.50 };
  9: export const DEFAULT_MATTR_BANDS = { low: 0.55, high: 0.70 };
 10: export const DEFAULT_MATTR_WINDOW = 50;
 11: export const DEFAULT_MIN_BURSTINESS_SENTENCES = 3;
 12: // KO-only "uniform plain-다 register" hot signal. AI Korean often writes short,
 13: // length-uniform prose in the flat declarative -다 register; formal human Korean
 14: // uses -다 too but with varied sentence lengths (high burstiness CV), and
 15: // conversational human Korean uses 요/습니다 (low -다 ratio). The signal therefore
 16: // fires only when low burstiness AND -다 dominance co-occur, which separates
 17: // AI-다 from formal-human-다 and conversational-human at short lengths.
 18: export const DEFAULT_KO_ENDING_MONOTONY = Object.freeze({ minDaRatio: 0.6, minDaCount: 2, minTokens: 20 });
 19: export const DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS = {
 20:   minSentences: 4,
 21:   minEojeols: 20,
 22:   spacing: {
 23:     maxEojeolLengthCV: 0.38,
 24:   },
 25:   comma: {
 26:     maxPerSentence: 1,
 27:   },
 28:   posProxy: {
 29:     minMatchedCount: 10,
 30:     maxClassDiversity: 0.26,
 31:   },
 32: };
 33: export const KO_POST_EDITESE_SCHEMA = 'koPostEditese.v1';
 34: 
 35: const HANGUL_RE = /[\u3131-\u318e\uac00-\ud7a3]/u;
 36: const COMMA_RE = /[,，、]/gu;
 37: 
 38: const KO_SUFFIX_GROUPS = [
 39:   { className: 'quote', suffixes: ['라고', '이라고'] },
 40:   { className: 'source', suffixes: ['에게서', '한테서', '으로부터', '로부터'] },
 41:   { className: 'instrument', suffixes: ['으로써', '로써'] },
 42:   { className: 'standard', suffixes: ['으로서', '로서'] },
 43:   { className: 'topic', suffixes: ['은', '는'] },
 44:   { className: 'subject', suffixes: ['이', '가', '께서'] },
 45:   { className: 'object', suffixes: ['을', '를'] },
 46:   { className: 'genitive', suffixes: ['의'] },
 47:   { className: 'location', suffixes: ['에서', '에게', '한테', '께', '에'] },
 48:   { className: 'direction', suffixes: ['으로', '로'] },
 49:   { className: 'conjunction', suffixes: ['와', '과', '하고', '랑'] },
 50:   { className: 'additive', suffixes: ['도', '또한'] },
 51:   { className: 'delimiter', suffixes: ['만', '까지', '부터', '마다'] },
 52:   { className: 'comparison', suffixes: ['보다', '처럼'] },
 53:   { className: 'formal_ending', suffixes: ['습니다', '습니까', '합니다', '합니까', '입니다'] },
 54:   { className: 'polite_ending', suffixes: ['어요', '아요', '예요', '이에요', '네요', '군요', '지요'] },
 55:   { className: 'casual_ending', suffixes: ['죠', '네', '군'] },
 56:   { className: 'declarative_ending', suffixes: ['한다', '된다', '했다', '였다', '이다', '있다', '없다'] },
 57: ];
 58: 
 59: const KO_SUFFIX_MATCHERS = KO_SUFFIX_GROUPS
 60:   .flatMap((group) =>
 61:     group.suffixes.map((suffix) => ({
 62:       className: group.className,
 63:       suffix,
 64:       length: Array.from(suffix).length,
 65:     }))
 66:   )
 67:   .sort((a, b) => b.length - a.length);
 68: 
 69: const POST_EDITESE_ENDING_SUFFIXES = [
 70:   '습니다', '습니까', '합니다', '합니까', '입니다', '어요', '아요', '예요', '이에요',
 71:   '네요', '군요', '지요', '죠', '한다', '된다', '했다', '였다', '이다', '있다',
 72:   '없다', '왔다', '봤다', '다',
 73: ].sort((a, b) => Array.from(b).length - Array.from(a).length);
 74: // Canonical token for regular formal '-ㅂ니다 / -ㅂ니까' endings (됩니다, 줍니다, 합니까…)
 75: // whose ㅂ marker fuses into the stem syllable and so isn't a literal suffix.
 76: const POST_EDITESE_FORMAL_NIDA = 'ㅂ니다';
 77: const POST_EDITESE_FORMAL_ENDINGS = new Set(['습니다', '습니까', '합니다', '합니까', '입니다', POST_EDITESE_FORMAL_NIDA]);
 78: const POST_EDITESE_POLITE_ENDINGS = new Set(['어요', '아요', '예요', '이에요', '네요', '군요', '지요', '죠']);
 79: const POST_EDITESE_DECLARATIVE_DA_ENDINGS = new Set(['한다', '된다', '했다', '였다', '이다', '있다', '없다', '왔다', '봤다', '다']);
 80: 
 81: // Bounded leading quantifier ({1,12}, not +): real Korean eojeols before a
 82: // relative-clause ending are short, but an unbounded greedy [가-힣]+ on a long
 83: // unspaced Hangul run backtracks one char per start position → O(n²) ReDoS that
 84: // runs unconditionally for ko on every analyzeText (and in the browser
 85: // playground on pasted text). The bound caps per-position work to a constant,
 86: // making the scan linear, without changing detection on normal prose (#502).
 87: const POST_EDITESE_RELATIVE_CLAUSE_PROXY_RE = /[가-힣]{1,12}(?:하는|되는|받는|받은|쓰인|되어진|보여진|한|할|된|될|던|운|온|인|힌|진|린|킨|쓴)\s+[가-힣]{1,12}/g;
 88: const POST_EDITESE_PROGRESSIVE_ASPECT_RE = /고\s*있(?:다|습니다|는|었|으|고|지|기)?/g;
 89: 
 90: function mean(values) {
 91:   if (!Array.isArray(values) || values.length === 0) return null;
 92:   return values.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / values.length;
 93: }
 94: 
 95: function coefficientOfVariation(values) {
 96:   if (!Array.isArray(values) || values.length < 2) return null;
 97:   const avg = mean(values);
 98:   if (!avg) return null;
 99:   const variance = values.reduce((acc, x) => acc + (x - avg) ** 2, 0) / values.length;
100:   return Math.sqrt(variance) / avg;
101: }
102: 
103: function cleanKoreanEojeol(chunk) {
104:   return chunk
105:     .normalize('NFC')
106:     .replace(/^[^\p{L}\p{N}]+|[^\p{L}\p{N}]+$/gu, '');
107: }
108: 
109: function koreanEojeols(paragraph) {
110:   if (!paragraph) return [];
111:   return paragraph
112:     .split(/\s+/u)
113:     .map(cleanKoreanEojeol)
114:     .filter((token) => HANGUL_RE.test(token));
115: }
116: 
117: function koreanLength(token) {
118:   return Array.from(token.replace(/[^\u3131-\u318e\uac00-\ud7a3]/gu, '')).length;
119: }
120: 
121: // Coefficient of variation of sentence token counts.
122: // Returns null when the paragraph has fewer than 2 sentences or mean is 0.
123: export function burstinessCV(sentenceTokenCounts) {
124:   if (!Array.isArray(sentenceTokenCounts) || sentenceTokenCounts.length < 2) return null;
125:   const n = sentenceTokenCounts.length;
126:   const mean = sentenceTokenCounts.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / n;
127:   if (mean === 0) return null;
128:   const variance =
129:     sentenceTokenCounts.reduce((acc, x) => acc + (x - mean) ** 2, 0) / n;
130:   return Math.sqrt(variance) / mean;
131: }
132: 
133: // Moving Average Type-Token Ratio (window default 50).
134: // Falls back to simple TTR when token count < window.
135: //
136: // Rolling implementation: keeps a running unique-token count over a sliding
137: // window (O(n)) instead of rebuilding a Set per window position (O(n*window)).
138: // This is bit-identical to the slice+Set form because each window's unique
139: // count equals `new Set(slice).size`, and the per-window terms `unique / window`
140: // are accumulated in the same left-to-right order, so `sum / count` is exact.
141: export function mattr(tokens, window = DEFAULT_MATTR_WINDOW) {
142:   if (!Array.isArray(tokens) || tokens.length === 0) return null;
143:   // A non-positive / non-integer window (e.g. a bad ttr.window in user YAML)
144:   // otherwise yields NaN or negative diversity that silently disables or
145:   // inverts the MATTR hot-signal document-wide (#507). Fall back to the
146:   // calibrated default rather than corrupting a benchmark-relevant signal.
147:   if (!Number.isInteger(window) || window <= 0) window = DEFAULT_MATTR_WINDOW;
148:   const lower = tokens.map((t) => t.toLowerCase());
149:   const n = lower.length;
150:   if (n < window) {
151:     return new Set(lower).size / n;
152:   }
153:   // Initialize the first window [0, window).
154:   const counts = new Map();
155:   let unique = 0;
156:   for (let i = 0; i < window; i++) {
157:     const tok = lower[i];
158:     const prev = counts.get(tok) || 0;
159:     if (prev === 0) unique++;
160:     counts.set(tok, prev + 1);
161:   }
162:   let sum = unique / window;
163:   let count = 1;
164:   // Slide one token at a time; window position i covers [i, i + window).
165:   for (let i = 1; i + window <= n; i++) {
166:     const out = lower[i - 1];
167:     const outCount = counts.get(out);
168:     if (outCount === 1) {
169:       counts.delete(out);
170:       unique--;
171:     } else {
172:       counts.set(out, outCount - 1);
173:     }
174:     const inc = lower[i + window - 1];
175:     const inCount = counts.get(inc) || 0;
176:     if (inCount === 0) unique++;
177:     counts.set(inc, inCount + 1);
178:     sum += unique / window;
179:     count++;
180:   }
181:   return sum / count;
182: }
183: 
184: export function koreanPostEditeseFeatures(text, opts = {}) {
185:   const lang = opts.lang ?? 'ko';
186:   const str = typeof text === 'string' ? text.normalize('NFC') : '';
187:   if (lang !== 'ko') return skippedKoPostEditese(lang, 'non-ko');
188:   if (str.trim().length === 0) return skippedKoPostEditese(lang, 'empty');
189: 
190:   const paragraphs = splitParagraphs(str);
191:   const totalEojeols = koreanEojeols(str);
192:   if (totalEojeols.length === 0) return skippedKoPostEditese(lang, 'no-hangul-eojeols');
193: 
194:   const paragraphRows = paragraphs.map((paragraph, index) => buildKoPostEditeseRow(paragraph, `P${index + 1}`));
195:   const docSentences = paragraphs.flatMap((paragraph) => splitProseSentences(paragraph));
196:   return {
197:     schema: KO_POST_EDITESE_SCHEMA,
198:     lang,
199:     analyzed: true,
200:     skipReason: null,
201:     paragraphCount: paragraphRows.length,
202:     sentenceCount: docSentences.length,
203:     eojeolCount: totalEojeols.length,
204:     metrics: buildKoPostEditeseMetrics(str, docSentences),
205:     paragraphs: paragraphRows,
206:   };
207: }
208: 
209: function skippedKoPostEditese(lang, skipReason) {
210:   return {
211:     schema: KO_POST_EDITESE_SCHEMA,
212:     lang,
213:     analyzed: false,
214:     skipReason,
215:     paragraphCount: 0,
216:     sentenceCount: 0,
217:     eojeolCount: 0,
218:     metrics: zeroKoPostEditeseMetrics(),
219:     paragraphs: [],
220:   };
221: }
222: 
223: function zeroKoPostEditeseMetrics() {
224:   return {
225:     lexical: {
226:       tokenCount: 0,
227:       typeCount: 0,
228:       ttr: null,
229:       mattr: null,
230:       endingTypeCount: 0,
231:       endingDiversity: null,
232:     },
233:     endings: {
234:       declarativeDaCount: 0,
235:       declarativeDaRatio: null,
236:       handaCount: 0,
237:       doendaCount: 0,
238:       idaCount: 0,
239:       formalEndingCount: 0,
240:       politeEndingCount: 0,
241:       endingStreakMax: 0,
242:     },
243:     interference: {
244:       pronounLiteralCount: 0,
245:       doubleParticleCount: 0,
246:       progressiveAspectCount: 0,
247:       lightVerbCount: 0,
248:       byPassiveCount: 0,
249:       doublePassiveCount: 0,
250:       connectiveCommaCount: 0,
251:       relativeClauseProxyCount: 0,
252:     },
253:     rhythm: {
254:       meanSentenceEojeols: null,
255:       sentenceEojeolCV: null,
256:       meanEojeolLength: null,
257:       eojeolLengthCV: null,
258:       commaPerSentence: null,
259:       commaPer100Chars: null,
260:       suffixMatchedCount: 0,
261:       suffixClassDiversity: null,
262:       suffixDiversity: null,
263:     },
264:   };
265: }
266: 
267: function buildKoPostEditeseRow(paragraph, id) {
268:   const sentences = splitProseSentences(paragraph);
269:   const eojeols = koreanEojeols(paragraph);
270:   return {
271:     id,
272:     sentenceCount: sentences.length,
273:     eojeolCount: eojeols.length,
274:     metrics: buildKoPostEditeseMetrics(paragraph, sentences),
275:   };
276: }
277: 
278: function buildKoPostEditeseMetrics(text, sentences = splitProseSentences(text)) {
279:   const eojeols = koreanEojeols(text);
280:   const lowerEojeols = eojeols.map((token) => token.toLowerCase());
281:   const typeCount = new Set(lowerEojeols).size;
282:   const lengths = eojeols.map(koreanLength).filter((length) => length > 0);
283:   const endings = sentences.map(extractSentenceEnding).filter(Boolean);
284:   const endingTypeCount = new Set(endings).size;
285:   const suffixStats = koPostEditeseSuffixStats(eojeols);
286:   const comma = commaDensity(text, sentences.length);
287:   const sentenceEojeolCounts = sentences
288:     .map((sentence) => koreanEojeols(sentence).length)
289:     .filter((count) => count > 0);
290: 
291:   return {
292:     lexical: {
293:       tokenCount: eojeols.length,
294:       typeCount,
295:       ttr: ratio(typeCount, eojeols.length),
296:       mattr: roundMetric(mattr(eojeols)),
297:       endingTypeCount,
298:       endingDiversity: ratio(endingTypeCount, sentences.length),
299:     },
300:     endings: buildKoPostEditeseEndings(endings),
301:     interference: {
302:       pronounLiteralCount: countPattern(text, buildKoInterferenceRegex('a16-pronoun-literal')),
303:       doubleParticleCount: countPattern(text, buildKoInterferenceRegex('a19-double-particle')),
304:       progressiveAspectCount: countPattern(text, POST_EDITESE_PROGRESSIVE_ASPECT_RE),
305:       lightVerbCount: countPattern(text, buildKoInterferenceRegex('a7-light-verb')),
306:       byPassiveCount: countPattern(text, buildKoInterferenceRegex('passive-e-uihae')),
307:       doublePassiveCount: countPattern(text, buildKoInterferenceRegex('a8-double-passive')),
308:       connectiveCommaCount: countPattern(text, buildKoInterferenceRegex('c11-connective-comma')),
309:       relativeClauseProxyCount: countPattern(text, POST_EDITESE_RELATIVE_CLAUSE_PROXY_RE),
310:     },
311:     rhythm: {
312:       meanSentenceEojeols: ratio(eojeols.length, sentences.length),
313:       sentenceEojeolCV: roundMetric(coefficientOfVariation(sentenceEojeolCounts)),
314:       meanEojeolLength: roundMetric(mean(lengths)),
315:       eojeolLengthCV: roundMetric(coefficientOfVariation(lengths)),
316:       commaPerSentence: roundMetric(comma.perSentence),
317:       commaPer100Chars: roundMetric(comma.per100Chars),
318:       suffixMatchedCount: suffixStats.matchedCount,
319:       suffixClassDiversity: roundMetric(suffixStats.classDiversity),
320:       suffixDiversity: roundMetric(suffixStats.suffixDiversity),
321:     },
322:   };
323: }
324: 
325: function buildKoPostEditeseEndings(endings) {
326:   const declarativeDaCount = endings.filter((ending) => POST_EDITESE_DECLARATIVE_DA_ENDINGS.has(ending)).length;
327:   return {
328:     declarativeDaCount,
329:     declarativeDaRatio: ratio(declarativeDaCount, endings.length),
330:     handaCount: endings.filter((ending) => ending === '한다').length,
331:     doendaCount: endings.filter((ending) => ending === '된다').length,
332:     idaCount: endings.filter((ending) => ending === '이다').length,
333:     formalEndingCount: endings.filter((ending) => POST_EDITESE_FORMAL_ENDINGS.has(ending)).length,
334:     politeEndingCount: endings.filter((ending) => POST_EDITESE_POLITE_ENDINGS.has(ending)).length,
335:     endingStreakMax: maxDeclarativeDaStreak(endings),
336:   };
337: }
338: 
339: // Per-paragraph declarative-다 ending monotony. This is a first-class hot-signal
340: // input (NOT the advisory koPostEditese payload): it reuses the deterministic
341: // ending classifier but is computed per paragraph and consumed by the hot
342: // decision in analyzeText. Returns { daCount, daRatio } (daRatio null on no endings).
343: export function koreanEndingMonotony(sentences = []) {
344:   const endings = sentences.map(extractSentenceEnding).filter(Boolean);
345:   const daCount = endings.filter((ending) => POST_EDITESE_DECLARATIVE_DA_ENDINGS.has(ending)).length;
346:   return { daCount, daRatio: ratio(daCount, endings.length) };
347: }
348: 
349: function extractSentenceEnding(sentence) {
350:   const eojeol = koreanEojeols(sentence).at(-1);
351:   if (!eojeol) return null;
352:   const matched = POST_EDITESE_ENDING_SUFFIXES.find((ending) => eojeol.endsWith(ending)) ?? null;
353:   // Regular formal '-ㅂ니다 / -ㅂ니까' (됩니다, 표시됩니다, 합니까…) fuse the ㅂ marker into
354:   // the stem syllable, so they fall through to the bare '다' bucket and get miscounted as
355:   // declarative '-다' style. Reclassify them as a formal ending.
356:   if ((matched === '다' || matched === null) && isFormalFusedEnding(eojeol)) {
357:     return POST_EDITESE_FORMAL_NIDA;
358:   }
359:   return matched;
360: }
361: 
362: // True for '-ㅂ니다 / -ㅂ니까' formal endings: the syllable before 니다/니까 carries a ㅂ
363: // jongseong (batchim index 17). Distinguishes 됩니다/아닙니다 (formal) from 아니다 (plain).
364: function isFormalFusedEnding(eojeol) {
365:   const m = /(.)(?:니다|니까)$/.exec(eojeol);
366:   if (!m) return false;
367:   const code = m[1].charCodeAt(0);
368:   if (code < 0xac00 || code > 0xd7a3) return false;
369:   return (code - 0xac00) % 28 === 17;
370: }
371: 
372: function maxDeclarativeDaStreak(endings) {
373:   let current = 0;
374:   let max = 0;
375:   for (const ending of endings) {
376:     if (POST_EDITESE_DECLARATIVE_DA_ENDINGS.has(ending)) {
377:       current += 1;
378:       max = Math.max(max, current);
379:     } else {
380:       current = 0;
381:     }
382:   }
383:   return max;
384: }
385: 
386: function koPostEditeseSuffixStats(eojeols) {
387:   const matches = [];
388:   for (const token of eojeols) {
389:     const match = KO_SUFFIX_MATCHERS.find(
390:       (candidate) => token.length > candidate.suffix.length && token.endsWith(candidate.suffix)
391:     );
392:     if (match) matches.push({ className: match.className, suffix: match.suffix });
393:   }
394:   const matchedCount = matches.length;
395:   const classCount = new Set(matches.map((match) => match.className)).size;
396:   const suffixCount = new Set(matches.map((match) => match.suffix)).size;
397:   return {
398:     matchedCount,
399:     classDiversity: matchedCount > 0 ? classCount / matchedCount : null,
400:     suffixDiversity: matchedCount > 0 ? suffixCount / matchedCount : null,
401:   };
402: }
403: 
404: function countPattern(text, pattern) {
405:   return (String(text ?? '').match(pattern) ?? []).length;
406: }
407: 
408: function ratio(numerator, denominator) {
409:   return denominator > 0 ? roundMetric(numerator / denominator) : null;
410: }
411: 
412: function roundMetric(value) {
413:   return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? Number(value.toFixed(3)) : null;
414: }
415: 
416: // Deterministic dominant-register detection for Korean prose. Maps sentence
417: // endings onto the three broad registers (formal -습니다, polite -요, plain
418: // -다) and reports the dominant one, or 'mixed' when nothing clears the
419: // threshold — register mixing across sentences is itself an AI tell that the
420: // rewrite prompt calls out. Used by the document-brief pipeline stage; this
421: // is an analysis-layer function and stays LLM-free.
422: const REGISTER_DOMINANCE_THRESHOLD = 0.6;
423: const REGISTER_LABELS = {
424:   formal: '합쇼체(-습니다)',
425:   polite: '해요체',
426:   plain: '평서체(-다)',
427:   mixed: '혼합',
428: };
429: 
430: export function detectKoreanRegister(text) {
431:   const paragraphs = splitParagraphs(String(text ?? ''));
432:   const sentences = paragraphs.flatMap((paragraph) => splitProseSentences(paragraph));
433:   // Register classification needs higher recall than the postEditese ending
434:   // sets (which are calibrated metrics inputs — widening them would shift the
435:   // analyzer benchmark). Suffix rules on the final eojeol: -니다/-니까 formal,
436:   // any -요/-죠 polite (covers 세요/나요/어요/까요…), remaining -다 plain.
437:   const finals = sentences
438:     .map((sentence) => koreanEojeols(sentence).at(-1) ?? '')
439:     .map((eojeol) => eojeol.replace(/[.!?…"'」』)\]]+$/g, ''))
440:     .filter(Boolean);
441:   const counts = { formal: 0, polite: 0, plain: 0 };
442:   for (const eojeol of finals) {
443:     if (/(?:니다|니까)$/.test(eojeol)) counts.formal += 1;
444:     else if (/[요죠]$/.test(eojeol)) counts.polite += 1;
445:     else if (/다$/.test(eojeol)) counts.plain += 1;
446:   }
447:   const classified = counts.formal + counts.polite + counts.plain;
448:   // Below three classified endings the distribution is noise, not a register.
449:   if (classified < 3) return null;
450:   const shares = {
451:     formal: counts.formal / classified,
452:     polite: counts.polite / classified,
453:     plain: counts.plain / classified,
454:   };
455:   const dominant = Object.keys(shares).reduce((a, b) => (shares[b] > shares[a] ? b : a));
456:   const register = shares[dominant] >= REGISTER_DOMINANCE_THRESHOLD ? dominant : 'mixed';
457:   return {
458:     register,
459:     label: REGISTER_LABELS[register],
460:     shares: {
461:       formal: Math.round(shares.formal * 100) / 100,
462:       polite: Math.round(shares.polite * 100) / 100,
463:       plain: Math.round(shares.plain * 100) / 100,
464:     },
465:     classified,
466:     sentenceCount: sentences.length,
467:   };
468: }
469: 
470: export function koreanSpacingFeatures(paragraph, eojeols = koreanEojeols(paragraph)) {
471:   const lengths = eojeols.map(koreanLength).filter((length) => length > 0);
472:   const eojeolCount = lengths.length;
473: 
474:   return {
475:     eojeolCount,
476:     meanEojeolLength: mean(lengths),
477:     eojeolLengthCV: coefficientOfVariation(lengths),
478:     singleSyllableRatio:
479:       eojeolCount > 0 ? lengths.filter((length) => length === 1).length / eojeolCount : null,
480:     longEojeolRatio:
481:       eojeolCount > 0 ? lengths.filter((length) => length >= 7).length / eojeolCount : null,
482:   };
483: }
484: 
485: export function commaDensity(paragraph, sentenceCount = null) {
486:   const commaCount = (paragraph.match(COMMA_RE) ?? []).length;
487:   const charCount = Array.from(paragraph.replace(/\s+/gu, '')).length;
488: 
489:   return {
490:     count: commaCount,
491:     perSentence: sentenceCount > 0 ? commaCount / sentenceCount : null,
492:     per100Chars: charCount > 0 ? (commaCount / charCount) * 100 : null,
493:   };
494: }
495: 
496: export function koreanPosDiversityProxy(paragraph, eojeols = koreanEojeols(paragraph)) {
497:   const matches = [];
498: 
499:   for (const token of eojeols) {
500:     const match = KO_SUFFIX_MATCHERS.find(
501:       (candidate) => token.length > candidate.suffix.length && token.endsWith(candidate.suffix)
502:     );
503:     if (match) {
504:       matches.push({ className: match.className, suffix: match.suffix });
505:     }
506:   }
507: 
508:   const matchedCount = matches.length;
509:   const classes = [...new Set(matches.map((match) => match.className))].sort();
510:   const suffixes = [...new Set(matches.map((match) => match.suffix))].sort();
511: 
512:   return {
513:     proxy: 'suffix',
514:     eojeolCount: eojeols.length,
515:     matchedCount,
516:     coverage: eojeols.length > 0 ? matchedCount / eojeols.length : null,
517:     distinctClassCount: classes.length,
518:     classDiversity: matchedCount > 0 ? classes.length / matchedCount : null,
519:     distinctSuffixCount: suffixes.length,
520:     suffixDiversity: matchedCount > 0 ? suffixes.length / matchedCount : null,
521:     classes,
522:   };
523: }
524: 
525: /**
526:  * @param {{ sentenceCount?: number, spacing?: object, comma?: object, posDiversity?: object }} [features]
527:  * @param {object} [bands]
528:  */
529: export function classifyKoreanDiagnostics({
530:   sentenceCount = 0,
531:   spacing,
532:   comma,
533:   posDiversity,
534: } = {}, bands = DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS) {
535:   const thresholds = mergeKoreanDiagnosticBands(bands);
536:   const reasons = [];
537: 
538:   const hasEnoughText =
539:     sentenceCount >= thresholds.minSentences &&
540:     (spacing?.eojeolCount ?? 0) >= thresholds.minEojeols;
541:   if (!hasEnoughText) {
542:     return { hot: false, strength: 0, reasons, thresholds };
543:   }
544: 
545:   const spacingStrength = lowThresholdStrength(
546:     spacing?.eojeolLengthCV,
547:     thresholds.spacing.maxEojeolLengthCV
548:   );
549:   if (spacingStrength > 0) reasons.push('regular-eojeol-length');
550: 
551:   const commaStrength = lowThresholdStrength(
552:     comma?.perSentence,
553:     thresholds.comma.maxPerSentence
554:   );
555:   if (commaStrength > 0) reasons.push('low-comma-density');
556: 
557:   const posHasCoverage =
558:     (posDiversity?.matchedCount ?? 0) >= thresholds.posProxy.minMatchedCount;
559:   const posStrength = posHasCoverage
560:     ? lowThresholdStrength(
561:         posDiversity?.classDiversity,
562:         thresholds.posProxy.maxClassDiversity
563:       )
564:     : 0;
565:   if (posStrength > 0) reasons.push('low-suffix-class-diversity');
566: 
567:   const componentStrengths = [spacingStrength, commaStrength, posStrength];
568:   const hot = componentStrengths.every((value) => value > 0);
569: 
570:   return {
571:     hot,
572:     strength: hot ? Math.min(...componentStrengths) : 0,
573:     reasons: hot ? reasons : [],
574:     thresholds,
575:   };
576: }
577: 
578: // Single-pass Korean per-paragraph diagnostics: tokenize eojeols ONCE and reuse
579: // them for the spacing and POS-diversity proxies (previously each recomputed
580: // koreanEojeols), then derive comma density and the composite classification.
581: // Output is identical to calling koreanSpacingFeatures/commaDensity/
582: // koreanPosDiversityProxy/classifyKoreanDiagnostics separately.
583: export function koreanDiagnostics(
584:   paragraph,
585:   sentenceCount,
586:   { enabled = true, bands = DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS } = {}
587: ) {
588:   const eojeols = koreanEojeols(paragraph);
589:   const spacing = koreanSpacingFeatures(paragraph, eojeols);
590:   const comma = commaDensity(paragraph, sentenceCount);
591:   const posDiversity = koreanPosDiversityProxy(paragraph, eojeols);
592:   const koDiagnostics = enabled
593:     ? classifyKoreanDiagnostics({ sentenceCount, spacing, comma, posDiversity }, bands)
594:     : { hot: false, strength: 0, reasons: [], thresholds: bands };
595:   return { spacing, comma, posDiversity, koDiagnostics };
596: }
597: 
598: export function classifyBurstiness(cv, bands = DEFAULT_BURSTINESS_BANDS) {
599:   if (cv == null) return null;
600:   if (cv < bands.low) return 'low';
601:   if (cv > bands.high) return 'high';
602:   return 'mid';
603: }
604: 
605: export function classifyMattr(value, bands = DEFAULT_MATTR_BANDS) {
606:   if (value == null) return null;
607:   if (value < bands.low) return 'low';
608:   if (value > bands.high) return 'high';
609:   return 'mid';
610: }
611: 
612: function mergeKoreanDiagnosticBands(bands = {}) {
613:   return {
614:     minSentences: resolveNumber(bands.minSentences, DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS.minSentences),
615:     minEojeols: resolveNumber(bands.minEojeols, DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS.minEojeols),
616:     spacing: {
617:       maxEojeolLengthCV: resolveNumber(
618:         bands.spacing?.maxEojeolLengthCV,
619:         DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS.spacing.maxEojeolLengthCV
620:       ),
621:     },
622:     comma: {
623:       maxPerSentence: resolveNumber(
624:         bands.comma?.maxPerSentence,
625:         DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS.comma.maxPerSentence
626:       ),
627:     },
628:     posProxy: {
629:       minMatchedCount: resolveNumber(
630:         bands.posProxy?.minMatchedCount,
631:         DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS.posProxy.minMatchedCount
632:       ),
633:       maxClassDiversity: resolveNumber(
634:         bands.posProxy?.maxClassDiversity,
635:         DEFAULT_KO_DIAGNOSTIC_BANDS.posProxy.maxClassDiversity
636:       ),
637:     },
638:   };
639: }
640: 
641: function resolveNumber(value, fallback) {
642:   return typeof value === 'number' && Number.isFinite(value) ? value : fallback;
643: }
644: 
645: function lowThresholdStrength(value, threshold) {
646:   if (typeof value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(value)) return 0;
647:   if (threshold === 0) return value <= 0 ? 100 : 0;
648:   if (!threshold || threshold < 0 || value > threshold) return 0;
649:   return Math.max(0, Math.min(100, (1 - value / threshold) * 100));
650: }
````
